(e.g., MATH consistently elicits more diverse error types than GSM8K/SVAMP), indicating that simple “valid” patterns cease to be
correct
on harder instances; (ii) many failures arise from
mis-applied common patterns
, such as
Assumed independence of overlapping events
(AIO),
Misapplication of probability formulas for independent events
(MPI),
Incorrect combinatorial principles
(ICP),
Unit/Conversion errors
(UNE/FAC), or
algebraic manipulation
mistakes (MAM), showing that widely used CoT routes are not instance-wise reliable; and (iii)
Error-Aware Prompting
(EAP) selectively diverts models from their default CoT routes and yields sizable per-category gains on hard cases (e.g., AIO
+6.1
pp, MPI
+6.5
pp, UNE
+6.5
pp, FAC
+13.5
pp), evidencing the value of rarer, problem-specific reasoning paths over frequent but brittle patterns.

This aligns with recent findings
74
;
37
;
48
;
69
highlighting the role of
reasoning diversity
and
entropy stability
in post-training, albeit evidence shows that post-training and inference-scaling do not explore beyond base model’s tree-search knowledge
81
;
3
;
23
.

Definition 4

(Formal Version of Def.
2
)
.

Let
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a Tree-structured Markov Chain (TMC) as defined in Def.
1
, and let
\mathcal{T}
be a collection of tasks. Each task
k\in\mathcal{T}
specifies a set of different question state
\mathcal{Q}_{k}\subset S_{1}
, where each
q\in\mathcal{Q}_{k}
has a corresponding unique correct answer
a_{q}^{k}
under task
k
. For
(q,a_{q}^{k})\neq(q^{\prime},a_{q^{\prime}}^{k})\in\mathcal{Q}_{k}
we have
q\neq q^{\prime}
,
a_{q}^{k}\neq a_{q^{\prime}}^{k}
.

A state tuple
(q,a_{q}=a_{q}^{k},k)
is called
common
if there exists at least one easy-to-reason chain of thought (CoT)
(o_{1},\dots,o_{L})
from
q
to
a_{q}
, and
rare
otherwise. Each such state tuple is associated with a set
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}\subset S_{1}\times\cdots\times S_{L}
of
valid
CoTs.

1.

All easy-to-reason CoTs from
q
to
a_{q}
belong to
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
;

2.

These CoTs are
not valid
for any task
k^{\prime}\neq k
;

3.

Hard-to-reason CoTs may or may not belong to
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
;

4.

Every edge
(o_{l}\to o_{l+1})
with non-zero transition probability appears in some valid CoT for some task;

5.

Each task state tuple
(q,a_{q},k)
induces a QA distribution
\mathcal{D}_{a}^{q,k}
, and the probability that a valid CoT
o_{1:L}\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
is
correct
for a concrete instance
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a}^{q,k}
is given by
p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)
:

p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)=\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}\mid o_{l})}{\sum_{o^{\prime}_{1:L}\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}\mid o^{\prime}_{l})}.

(32)

Further, we assume that the correctness of any different
o\neq o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
for any instance
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a}^{q,k}
is independent.

A task
k
is denoted rare if there is no valid easy-to-reason CoT in its
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
for any question-answer state pair
(a,a_{q})\in\mathcal{Q}_{k}
, and common other wise.

Remark 6
.

Here, the fifth condition is to provide merits for the probability distribution of the original TMC
X
(
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}
) that models after real-world LLM. Typically, the predictive distribution obtained from pretraining would match the “frequency” of whether a CoT be valid for certain task. That is, through the 5-th condition, we justify why the original TMC
X
(
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}
) would be equipped its distribution–driven by inherent chance to become a valid CoT for some reasoning task.

The independence assumption in the 5-th condition is for technical convenience. This definition would also induces instance
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})
under task
k
that has no correct CoT, with probability
\prod_{o\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k)}}(1-p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o))
.
In real-world, the situation is far more complex, and we left the consideration of theory that assumes the interaction and co-relationship of the correctness of CoTs with different difficulty level for future work.

Some examples of Valid-not-Correct:

For some task tuple
(o_{1},a_{o_{1}},k)
, denote
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k)}
as the subset of valid easy-to-reason CoTs inside the valid CoTs for task
k
, and
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}:=\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k)}\setminus\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
the subset of valid hard-to-reason CoTs. For any sampled instance
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a}^{q,k}
, it has the following two scenarios:

•

With probability
\prod_{o\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}(1-p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o))
, the
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})
can only be correctly solved by some valid hard-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}
.

•

With probability
1-\prod_{o\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}(1-p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o))
, the
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})
can be correctly solved by some easy-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
.

This division of probability space would equip bounding the pass@K performance when the model is only capable of all valid easy-to-reason CoTs. After the finetuned model is also capable of the hard-to-reason CoTs in
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}
, we turn to be interested in the following division of probability space to discuss the pass@K performance:

•

With probability
\prod_{o\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}}(1-p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o))
, the
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})
can only be correctly solved by some
unlearned
valid hard-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}\setminus\widetilde{\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}
.

•

With probability
1-\prod_{o\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}}(1-p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o))
, the
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})
can be correctly solved by some easy-to-reason CoTs or learned valid hard-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}
.

We can characterize the breadth of tasks encoded in the topology of TMC as follows.

Corollary 6

(Cardinality of Multi-task TMC)
.

Let
M_{0}=|S_{1}|
,
M_{L}=|S_{L}|
, and for each
q\in S_{1}
define

A(q)=\bigl\{\,a\in S_{L}:\exists\text{ \emph{easy-to-reason} CoT }q\rightsquigarrow a\,\bigr\},\qquad R(q)=\bigl\{\,a\in S_{L}:\exists\text{ CoT }q\rightsquigarrow a\,\bigr\}.

By Def.
1
, we have
|A(q)|=\mathrm{n}_{q}=O(1)
, and
\mathrm{n}_{q}\geq 2
for all
q
. Define the set of all tasks as

\mathcal{T}=\{\,k:S_{1}\to S_{L}\,\},\qquad\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{common}}=\{\,k:k(q)\in A(q)\text{ for all }q\,\},\qquad\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rare}}=\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{common}}.

Then

|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{common}}|=\prod_{q\in S_{1}}|A(q)|=\Theta(c^{M_{0}}),\qquad 2\leq c\leq\max_{q}\mathrm{n}_{q}=O(1),

and

|\mathcal{T}|\leq\prod_{q\in S_{1}}|R(q)|\leq M_{L}^{M_{0}},\qquad|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{rare}}|=|\mathcal{T}|-\Theta(c^{M_{0}}).

In particular, although the total number of tasks grows exponentially in
M_{0}
, the number of
common
tasks is exponentially smaller whenever
M_{L}\gg c
.

Proof.
Proof of Lemma
6
. Each task
k\in\mathcal{T}
is a function
k:S_{1}\to S_{L}
, so
|\mathcal{T}|\leq\prod_{q\in S_{1}}|R(q)|
, where
R(q)
contains all reachable answers (via any CoT) from
q
. The set
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{common}}
consists of tasks for which
k(q)\in A(q)
for all
q
, so

|\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{common}}|=\prod_{q\in S_{1}}|A(q)|=\prod_{q\in S_{1}}\mathrm{n}_{q}=\Theta(c^{M_{0}}),

with
c\in[2,\max_{q}\mathrm{n}_{q}]=O(1)
. The rest follows directly by subtraction.
∎

Lemma 2
.

Consider a TMC
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
defined in Def.
1
, and a specific task
k\in\mathcal{T}
defined in Def.
2
. Then, for any fixed
q=o_{1}\in S_{1}
and corresponding correct answer
a=o_{L}\in S_{L}
, with non-trivial probability, there exists at least one hard-to-reason CoT trajectory (i.e., a path containing at least one sparse edge) from
q
to
a
. Specifically, the probability of having at least one such hard-to-reason trajectory, denoted
\mathbb{P}_{\text{deg}}(o_{L}=a|o_{1}=q)
, is lower bounded as:

\mathbb{P}_{\text{deg}}(o_{L}=a|o_{1}=q)\geq\Theta(\epsilon\cdot M^{L-3})\geq c\geq\Theta(M^{-2})>0,

for some constant
c
, where
\epsilon=O(1/M^{L-2})
is the transition probability of a sparse edge.

Proof of Lemma
2
.

Fix
q=o_{1}
and
a=o_{L}
. Let
\Pi
be the set of all length
-
L
trajectories
\tau=(o_{1},\dots,o_{L})
with
o_{L}=a
. We split
\Pi=\Pi_{\rm nd}\cup\Pi_{\rm deg}
according to whether
\tau
has zero or at least one sparse edge.

By Def.
1
, there exist
O(1)
“easy-to-reason” trajectories from
q\in S_{1}
to
a\in S_{L}
, each consisting entirely of high-probability transitions
C_{o_{l}}
.
Each transition
o_{l}\to o_{l+1}
along these paths has probability
\Theta(1/M)
.
Therefore, for a trajectory of
L-1
steps, the total probability of such a path is:

\mathbb{P}_{\text{high}}=O(1)\cdot\left(\Theta\left(\frac{1}{M}\right)\right)^{L-1}=O\left(M^{-(L-1)}\right).

Similarly, as the number of hard-to-reason CoT is below
\Theta(M)
, given that
\mathbb{P}_{\text{sparse}}\leq\Theta(1/M^{L-2})
, we conclude the total probability by union bound
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{L}=a\mid o_{1}=q)=\Theta(M^{-(L-1)})
.

∎

Theorem 7

(Intrinsic Properties of Multi-task TMC)
.

Let
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a Tree-structured Markov Chain (TMC) and
\mathcal{T}
a set of tasks, per defined in Def.
1
and
2
.

1.

(Task Interference)

Let tasks
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}
share at least one question state
q\in S_{1}
or answer state
a\in S_{L}
, with distinct valid QA pairs
(q,a_{q})\in\mathcal{Q}_{k}
and
(q^{\prime},a_{q^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{Q}_{k^{\prime}}
. Suppose the transition probabilities along edges in
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
are amplified such that the TMC reaches
a_{q}
with probability
1-\delta
(where
\delta=o(M^{-L})<<1
) via valid CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
. Then for all shared
q
or
a
, every originally easy-to-reason CoT in
\mathcal{G}_{q^{\prime},a_{q^{\prime}}}^{(k^{\prime})}
must satisfy:

\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})=o(1/M^{2})\quad\exists(o_{l}\to o_{l+1})\in\tau\text{ and }\tau\in\mathcal{G}_{q^{\prime},a_{q^{\prime}}}^{(k^{\prime})},

i.e., all such CoTs degenerate into hard-to-reason paths. Similarly, for any task
\hat{k}\neq k\in\mathcal{T}
whose valid CoT set
\mathcal{G}_{\hat{q},a_{\hat{q}}}^{(\hat{k})}
has at least one easy-to-reason CoT
\hat{o}
sharing some transitions
\hat{o}_{l}\to\hat{o}_{l+1}
with the CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
. Then
\hat{o}
becomes hard-to-reason.

2.

(Correctness Bottleneck)

Suppose the probability mass of valid hard-to-reason CoTs traveling from
q
to
a_{q}
for task
k
in the original TMC
X
(
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}
) is
\Delta
.

Then suppose a model
\hat{p}
satisfies:

•

The total probability mass from
q
to
a
is
1-C
.

•

The fraction of easy-to-reason CoTs among CoTs traveling from
q
to
a_{q}
is
1-\epsilon
.

Then the expected correctness over the QA distribution
\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{q,k}
is upper bounded by:

\displaystyle{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{\hat{p}}(\bm{o})\leq\Theta((1-C)[{(1-\epsilon)\frac{1}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}}+\epsilon\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}}}])

Besides, we denote the pass@K performance of model
\hat{p}
for task tuple
(q,a_{q},k)
(the probability that at least succeed once among
K
trials) as
\mathrm{Pass@K}_{q,k}^{\hat{p}}
:

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{q,k}^{\hat{p}}:=\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{\bm{o}^{i}\}_{i\in[K]}\sim\hat{p}(O\mid q)\\
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{q,k}\end{subarray}}[\bigcup_{i=1}^{K}\mathds{1}\bigl(\bm{o}^{i}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{(k)}\bigr)].

(33)

When
C=0
,
\mathrm{Pass@K}_{q,k}^{\hat{p}}
is upper bounded by

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{q,k}^{\hat{p}}\leq\underbrace{\Theta([(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}(1-(1-\varepsilon)^{K})])}_{\text{upper bound of pass@K of instance that cannot be solved by easy CoTs}}+\underbrace{\Theta([(1-(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})(1-\varepsilon^{K})])}_{\text{upper bound of pass@K of instance that can be solved by some easy CoT}}).

If

\varepsilon=o(\sqrt[K]{1-C_{\mathrm{Err}}/(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}}))

for some
C_{\mathrm{Err}}\in(0,(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})
, then we have the pass@K performance upper bounded by

1-\Omega(C_{\mathrm{Err}})=o(1),

with
constant error

\Omega(C_{\mathrm{Err}})=\Theta(1)
.

When
C=\epsilon=0
, we have

{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{\hat{p}}(\bm{o})\leq\Theta(\frac{1}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})

And the pass@K performance is upper bounded by

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{q,k}^{\hat{p}}\leq\Theta(1-(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}).

Proof.
Proof of Thm.
7
.

1. non-negligible decay of transitions for other tasks when overfit a target task.

Fix a shared question state
q\in S_{1}
. By Def.
2
(ii), the easy-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
and
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q^{\prime}}}^{(k^{\prime})}
are disjoint. The amplification condition implies:

\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}}\mathbb{P}(\tau|q)\geq 1-\delta=1-o(M^{-L}).

Since
\sum_{\tau\in\text{all CoTs from }q}\mathbb{P}(\tau|q)=1
, the remaining CoTs (including those in
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q^{\prime}}}^{(k^{\prime})}
) must satisfy:

\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q^{\prime}}}^{(k^{\prime})}}\mathbb{P}(\tau|q)\leq\delta=o(M^{-L})<<1.

For any easy-to-reason CoT
\tau=(q,o_{2},\dots,o_{L}=a_{q^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q^{\prime}}}^{(k^{\prime})}
, the original transition probabilities satisfy
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})=\Theta(1/M)
for all edges. However, since the total probability mass for
\tau
is now
o(M^{-L})
, we have:

\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})=o(M^{-L}).

Give
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})\leq\Theta(1/M)
, this forces at least one transition term to decay to
o(1/M^{2})
. Otherwise, if any edge retained
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})=\Theta(1/M)
, the product would be
\Theta(M^{-(L-1)})
, contradicting
\mathbb{P}(\tau|q)=o(M^{-L})
.

For a shared answer state
a\in S_{L}
, as well as
\hat{o}
in othe task sharing some transition with CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
, the same logic applies.

2. non-negligible error when only favoring easy-to-learn CoTs.

We have the total mass of the valid CoTs for task
k
in the original
X
as

Z\geq\underbrace{\Theta(\frac{1}{M^{L-1}})}_{Z_{\text{easy}}}+\underbrace{\Delta}_{Z_{\text{hard}}}

By Def.
2
, where the expected correctness over a QA sample is proportion to the CoT’s likelihood in the original
X
, we can combine the components to upper bound the expected correctness:

\displaystyle{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{\hat{p}}(\bm{o})=\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{q,k}\\
\bm{o}\sim\hat{p}(\cdot\mid q)\end{subarray}}[\mathds{1}(\bm{o}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{(k)})]\leq

\displaystyle\Theta((1-C)[{(1-\epsilon)\frac{1}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}}+\epsilon\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}}}]).

Especially, by the first discussion of division of probability space in Remark
6
, it is direct to deduce that the probability that one specific
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{q,k}
cannot be solved by every easy-to-reason CoT is

\prod_{o\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}(1-p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o))=\Theta((1-\frac{1}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})=\Theta((\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})

When facing these instances, we have the probability of success to be
at most

\varepsilon
when
C=0
.

Besides, the probability that
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{q,k}
can be solved by some easy-to-reason CoT is

1-\Theta((\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})

When facing these instances, we have the probability of success to be
at most

1-\varepsilon
when
C=0
.

Therefore, collaborating with
\mathrm{n}_{q}=O(1),\forall q\in S_{1}
, the pass@K performance (the probability that at least succeed once among
K
trials) is upper bounded by

\underbrace{\Theta([(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}(1-(1-\varepsilon)^{K})])}_{\text{upper bound of pass@K of instance that cannot be solved by easy CoTs}}+\underbrace{\Theta([(1-(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})(1-\varepsilon^{K})])}_{\text{upper bound of pass@K of instance that can be solved by some easy CoT}}).

If

\varepsilon=o(\sqrt[K]{1-C_{\mathrm{Err}}/(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}})),

for some
C_{\mathrm{Err}}\in{(0,(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})}
, then we have the pass@K performance upper bounded by
1-\Omega(C_{\mathrm{Err}})
with constant error
\Theta(C_{\mathrm{Err}})=\Theta(1)
.

When
C=\epsilon=0
, we have
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\leq\Theta(\frac{1}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})
.
The pass@K performance is upper bounded by

\Theta(1-(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}).

∎

Lemma 3

(Formal Version of Prop.
1
)
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
that exact predicts the distribution of a Multi-task TMC as in Def.
1
and
2
, fix a common task state tuple
(q,a,k)
. For any valid easy-to-reason CoT
o^{\text{easy}}
and hard-to-learn CoT
o^{\text{hard}}
that share the states
o_{l}
, and deviate at the
l+1
layer (i.e.,
{o}_{l}^{\text{easy}}={o}_{l}^{\text{hard}}=o_{l}
,
{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\neq{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}
), if the total number of valid hard-to-reason CoTs is bounded by
\Theta(M)
, we have
A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}})\geq c_{1}>0,\ A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}})\leq-c_{2}<0,\forall l\in[L-1]
for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
.

Proof of Lemma
3
.

First, it is direct to see that given any valid easy-to-learn CoT
o^{\text{easy}}\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
and hard-to-learn CoT
o^{\text{hard}}\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
for task tuple
(q,a,k)
, we have

\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\mid o_{l})}{\sum_{o^{\prime}_{1:L}\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}\mid o^{\prime}_{l})}/\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\mid o_{l})}{\sum_{o^{\prime}_{1:L}\in\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}\mid o^{\prime}_{l})}\geq\frac{\Theta(M^{-1})}{o(M^{-1})}>\Omega(M),

(34)

by Eq.(
32
).

That is, the expected accuracy of
o^{\text{easy}}
on any instance from
\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{q,k}
, denoted as
p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o^{\text{easy}})
is larger than the
o^{\text{hard}}
, denoted as
p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o^{\text{hard}})
, with a ratio no less than
\Theta(M)
.

Fix
l
and
o_{l}
. Write

Q^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}})=\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=o_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}_{k})\\
\bm{o}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}[\mathds{1}({o}_{L}=a_{q})p_{\text{acc}}^{k}({o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}],

and

V^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})Q^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}).

By definition and Lemma
2
there are
\Theta(1)
length-
(L-l)
continuations each with probability
\Theta(M^{-(L-l)})
. Hence

Q^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}})=\Theta\bigl(M^{-(L-l)}\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]\bigr),

Also we see
o_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\in C_{o_{l}}
. Then
\Pr[o_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\mid o_{l}]=\Theta(1/M)
, thus

V^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l})\geq\Theta\bigl(M^{-(L-l+1)}\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]\bigr).

Therefore, we have

A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}})=Q^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}})-V^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l})\geq\Theta\bigl(M^{-(L-l+1)}\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]\bigr)>0

o_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\notin C_{o_{l}}
. Then
\Pr[o_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\mid o_{l}]=o(M^{-2})
, and the best possible continuations contribute at most
\Theta(M^{-(L-l-1)})
each. Thus

\displaystyle Q^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}})

\displaystyle\leq o(M^{-2})\cdot O\bigl(M^{-(L-l-1)}\bigr)\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]

\displaystyle=o\bigl(M^{-(L-l+1)}\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]\bigr).

Therefore

\displaystyle A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}})

\displaystyle=Q_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}})-V^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle=o\bigl(M^{-(L-l+1)}\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]\bigr)-\Theta\bigl(M^{-(L-l+1)}\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]\bigr)

\displaystyle<0.

Given that for a chosen TMC
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(\cdot\mid\cdot)=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\cdot|\cdot)
, the
\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}],\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mid\begin{subarray}{c}{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]
are constants. Therefore, by choosing some positive constants
c_{1}=\Theta(M^{-(L+1-l)}),c_{2}=\Theta(M^{-(L+1-l)})
to bound the advantages, we complete the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Details and Proofs of Pretraining

Following
33
, we could have the following theorem.

Theorem 8
.

Let
X_{0}\sim\operatorname{Unif}(S\setminus S_{L})
and
X_{1}\sim\mathbb{P}(\cdot|X_{0})
be random samples from the TMC
X
in Def.
1
. Let
X_{0}\sim\operatorname{Unif}(S\setminus S_{L})
and
X_{1}\sim\mathbb{P}(\cdot|X_{0})
be random samples from the TMC
X
defined in Def.
1
. For
i\in S_{l}
, define:

D_{o_{l}}=\{o_{l+1}:\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})>0\},\quad c=\min_{o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})>0,

then the softmax predictor trained via Algorithm
1
satisfies:

1.

After
T
iterations with
\eta=\Theta(M)
, the uniform convergence rate is:

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}l\in[L-1],o_{l}\in S_{l}\\
o_{l+1}\in S_{l+1}\end{subarray}}|\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})-\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})|\leq\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{M}{T}}\right)

(35)

where
\widetilde{O}
hides
\log(TMc^{-1})
factors.

2.

For threshold
c_{\mathrm{thres}}=\Theta(1)
, after
T_{1}=\widetilde{\Theta}(M^{2}c^{-2})
steps:

\begin{cases}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=0&\text{if }\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})=0\\
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})-\widetilde{O}(c)\leq\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})+\widetilde{O}(c)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(36)

3.

Post-thresholding, linear convergence occurs:

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(o_{l},o_{l+1})\in\\
\mathrm{supp}(\mathbb{P})\end{subarray}}|\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{(T_{1}+T)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})-\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})|\leq\widetilde{O}(e^{-\Omega(c^{2}T)})

(37)

Remark 9
.

The logarithmic factors in
\widetilde{O}
terms explicitly track:

•

\log(T_{1})=\log(M^{2}c^{-2})
for thresholding

•

\log(c^{-1})
for initialization dependence

•

\log M
for high-probability transition

Since we are considering a vanilla regression setting, the proof is standard following
33
;
30
. For the convenience of readers, we provide the proof here.

Algorithm 1

Pretraining of Foundation Model
33

1:

set
{\bm{\theta}}^{(0)}=\bm{0}
,
\eta=O(M)
,

2:

T_{1}=\widetilde{O}(M^{2}c^{-2})
,
T_{2}=\widetilde{O}(Mc^{-2})

3:

for

t=1,\cdots,T_{1}

do

4:

{\bm{\theta}}^{(t)}={\bm{\theta}}^{(t-1)}+\eta\nabla\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{1}}[\log\hat{p}_{{\bm{\theta}}^{(t-1)}}(X_{1}|X_{0})]

5:

end

for

6:

{\bm{\theta}}_{ij}^{(T_{1})}\leftarrow-\infty
if
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})^{(T_{1})}<c_{\mathrm{thres}}
{thresholding}

7:

for

t-T_{1}=1,\cdots,T_{2}

do

8:

{\bm{\theta}}^{(t)}={\bm{\theta}}^{(t-1)}+\eta\nabla\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{1}}[\log\hat{p}_{{\bm{\theta}}^{(t-1)}}(X_{1}|X_{0})]

9:

end

for

Proof.
We analyze each part of Thm.
8
systematically.

Proof of Item
1
: Uniform Convergence
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{l,l+1}=\{(o_{l},o_{l+1}):o_{l}\in S_{l},o_{l+1}\in S_{l+1}\}
denote all potential transitions. For each
(o_{l},o_{l+1})\in\mathcal{E}_{l,l+1}
, define the parameter error
\Delta^{(t)}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})-\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})
. For a given state
o_{l}
, the cross-entropy loss is:

L_{o_{l}}(\bm{\theta})=-\sum_{o_{l+1}\in S_{l+1}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})

where the model’s predicted probability is:

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=\frac{e^{\bm{\theta}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}}}{\sum_{o^{\prime}_{l+1}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{o_{l},o^{\prime}_{l+1}}}}

Similar to Lemma
6
, the gradient component for parameter
\bm{\theta}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}
is:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}}L_{o_{l}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{o^{\prime}_{l+1}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}|o_{l}){\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}^{\prime}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}

(38)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{o^{\prime}_{l+1}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}|o_{l})\frac{\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}^{\prime}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}^{\prime}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}

(39)

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})\frac{\nabla\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}-\sum_{o^{\prime}_{l+1}\neq o_{l+1}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}|o_{l})\frac{\nabla\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}^{\prime}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}^{\prime}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}

(40)

Using the softmax derivative property:

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}^{\prime}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=\begin{cases}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})(1-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))&\text{if }o^{\prime}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}\\
-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}^{\prime}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})&\text{if }o^{\prime}_{l+1}\neq o_{l+1}\end{cases}

(41)

Substituting these derivatives yields:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}}L_{o_{l}}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})(1-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))+\sum_{o^{\prime}_{l+1}\neq o_{l+1}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}|o_{l})\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})

(42)

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})+\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\underbrace{\sum_{o^{\prime}_{l+1}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}|o_{l})}_{=1}

(43)

\displaystyle=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})-\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})

(44)

Then the gradient descent update rule is:

\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}=\bm{\theta}^{(t-1)}_{o_{l},o_{l+1}}+\eta\left(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{(t-1)}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})\right)

This corresponds to the classical softmax parameter updates. The key challenge lies in the heterogeneous transition probabilities:

•

For
o_{l+1}\in C_{o_{l}}
:
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})=\Theta(1/M)
, with
|C_{o_{l}}|\leq M

•

For
o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus C_{o_{l}}
:
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})\geq c
but
o(1/M)

•

For
o_{l+1}\notin D_{o_{l}}
:
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})=0

Phase 1 - High-probability edges: Let
M_{0}=|S_{1}|=\Theta(M)
. The initial parameters
\bm{\theta}^{(0)}=0
yield uniform distribution:

\hat{p}^{(0)}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=\frac{1}{|S_{l+1}|}\leq\frac{1}{M_{0}}=O(1/M)

For
o_{l+1}\in C_{o_{l}}
, the initial error is
\Theta(1/M)-O(1/M)=\Theta(1/M)
. Each gradient step updates
\hat{p}
by
\eta\cdot\Theta(1/M)
. To reach
\epsilon
-accuracy for these edges, we need
T\geq\Omega(M^{2}/\epsilon^{2})
.

Phase 2 - Low-probability edges: For
o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus C_{o_{l}}
, the signal-to-noise ratio is weaker. The gradient signal is
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})-\hat{p}\geq c-O(1/M)
. Using the regret bound for online gradient descent
(
26
, Theorem 3.1)
:

\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\hat{p}^{(t)}-\mathbb{P})^{2}\leq O\left(\frac{\log|S_{l+1}|}{\eta}+\eta Tc^{2}\right)

Optimizing
\eta
yields
T\geq\widetilde{\Omega}(M/(c^{2}\epsilon^{2}))
for
\epsilon
-accuracy. Combining both phases via union bound over
O(M)
edges per layer and
O(L)=O(1)
layers gives:

\sup|\Delta^{(T)}|\leq O\left(\sqrt{\frac{M\log T}{T}}\cdot\log\left(\frac{TM}{c}\right)\right)

This matches Equation equation
35
after constant absorption.

Proof of Item
2
: Support Recovery via Thresholding
. After
T_{1}=\widetilde{O}(KM^{2}c^{-2})
iterations:

•

Zero-probability edges: For
o_{l+1}\notin D_{o_{l}}
, the true probability
\mathbb{P}=0
. The empirical estimate satisfies:

\hat{p}^{(T_{1})}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\log(1/\delta)}{T_{1}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{M}\right)

via Azuma’s inequality for martingales. Setting
\delta=c_{\text{thres}}c
and
T_{1}\geq\widetilde{\Omega}(M^{2}c^{-2})
ensures
\hat{p}\leq c_{\text{thres}}c
.

•

Non-zero edges: From Item
1
, for
o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}
:

|\hat{p}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbb{P}|\leq O\left(\sqrt{\frac{M}{T_{1}}}\log T_{1}\right)=o(c)

Thus
\hat{p}^{(T_{1})}\geq\mathbb{P}-o(c)\geq c-o(c)>c_{\text{thres}}c
for proper
c_{\text{thres}}<1
.

Thresholding at
c_{\text{thres}}c
thus exactly recovers the support while maintaining Equation equation
36
.

Proof of Item
3
: Linear Convergence
. Post-thresholding, the parameter space restricts to
D_{o_{l}}
edges. The Hessian of
L_{\mathrm{pre}}
becomes:

\nabla^{2}L_{\mathrm{pre}}(\bm{\theta})_{(o_{l},o_{l+1}),(o_{l},o_{l+1}^{\prime})}=\text{Cov}(\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}),\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l}))

Under the TMC structure, the Fisher information matrix
I(\bm{\theta})
satisfies
\lambda_{\min}(I)\geq\Omega(c^{2})
since all active transitions have probability
\geq c
. By
(
30
, Theorem 4.1)
, gradient descent on strongly convex objectives achieves:

\|\Delta^{(T_{1}+t)}\|^{2}\leq\exp(-\Omega(c^{2}t))\|\Delta^{(T_{1})}\|^{2}

Given
\|\Delta^{(T_{1})}\|=O(\sqrt{M/T_{1}}\log T_{1})=O(\log c^{-1})
from Item
2
, we obtain Equation equation
37
.

∎

Appendix G
Details and Proofs of RLVR Finetuning

During the gradient update, for any
o_{l}\in S_{l},l\in[L-1]
that appears as the transition in the valid CoT set
\cup_{q\in\mathcal{Q}_{k}}\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
for task
k\in\mathcal{T}
, we define the following notations (summarized in Table
4
)

•

\mathcal{I}_{o_{l+1},o_{l}}^{(k)}\subseteq\cup_{q\in\mathcal{Q}_{k}}\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
as the subset of valid CoTs satisfies
\forall o^{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{o_{l+1},o_{l}}^{(k)}
,
o_{l}^{i}=o_{l},o_{l+1}^{i}=o_{l+1}
.

•

\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}:=\{o_{l+1}\in S_{l}\mid\mathcal{I}_{o_{l+1},o_{l}}^{(k)}\neq\emptyset\}\subseteq S_{l+1}
as the subset of
l+1
-th layer states collecting the states such that for any valid CoT
o
for task
k
passing
o_{l}^{\prime}
,
o_{l+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}^{\prime}}^{(k)}
.

•

\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
contains the subset of
l+1
-th layer’s states in
\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
passed by at least one easy-to-reason CoTs (in the original TMC
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}
) for task
k
, and
\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}}:=\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
contains the
l+1
-th layer’s states only passed by valid hard-to-reason CoTs for task
k
.

•

\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k)}
as the subset of valid easy-to-reason CoTs inside the valid CoTs for task
k
, and
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}:=\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k)}\setminus\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
the subset of valid hard-to-reason CoTs.

•

\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}
be the finetuned model for the task
k
at post-training iteration
t

•

For any CoT
o\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k)}
, the probability that
o
is correct for a sampled instance
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{o_{1}}}^{o_{1},k}
is given by
p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)
, which, per Condition (iv) in Def.
2
, is proportional to its likelihood among
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k)}
:

p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)=\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}\mid o_{l})}{\sum_{o^{\prime}_{1:L}\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k)}}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o^{\prime}_{l+1}\mid o^{\prime}_{l})},

where
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(\cdot\mid o_{l})=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\cdot\mid\bm{o}_{l}),\ o_{l}\in S_{l}
, is the transition kernel of our Multi-task TMC.

•

The gradient update objectives
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})
and
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})
represent

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{o_{1}}}^{o_{1},k},\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}\left[R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k}(\bm{o})\right],

(45)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{o_{1}}}^{o_{1},k},\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k}(\bm{o})\right].

•

The objective of
RL-rej

\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{Rein-rej}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})
, in our case, is training using the REINFORCE objective on a online distorted data distribution
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{rej,(t)}}^{o_{1},k}
, formally

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{Rein-rej}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{rej,(t)}}^{o_{1},k},\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}\left[R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k}(\bm{o})\right].

(46)

Here,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{rej,(t)}}^{o_{1},k}:=\{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{o_{1}}}^{o_{1},k}\mid\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot\mid o_{1})\end{subarray}}[\bigcup_{i=1}^{G}\mathds{1}\bigl(\bm{o}^{i}\notin\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{(k)}\bigr)]=\Theta(1)\}
, where
G=O(1)
is the (offset) time of parallel experiments. That is, the algorithm rejects samples with
\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot\mid o_{1})\end{subarray}}[\bigcap_{i=1}^{G}\mathds{1}\bigl(\bm{o}^{i}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{(k)}\bigr)=\Theta(1)
, which represents instances that the model confidently predicts its correct CoTs of
G
times in parallel. Therefore, idealistically, instance sampled from
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{rej,(t)}}^{o_{1},k}
would have some correct CoTs that is not well-learned by the current model
\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}
.

Theorem 10

(Squeezing Effect and Merits of Rejecting Correct (Full Version of Thm.
2
and Cor.
2
))
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
that exact predicts the distribution of a Multi-task TMC as in Def.
1
and
2
, and
\bm{\theta}^{k}
the current model to be finetuned from
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
for task
k\in\mathcal{T}
. Denote the task tuples of task
k\in\mathcal{T}
as
(q,a_{q}^{k},k)
, where
a_{q}^{k}\in S_{L}
is the sole answer state under task
k
. Assume for each
(o_{1},a_{o_{1}},k)
under task
k
, the number of hard-to-reason CoTs from
o_{1}
to
a_{o_{1}}
is bounded by
O(M)
. Let the question distribution during finetuning of task
k
be
P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})
(i.e.,
o_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})
). Then, when finetuning the base model using REINFORCE and RAFT objectives in Eq.(
45
), we have

1.

Squeezing Effect
\&
Difference of Logit Update
. For any different state pair
{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\neq{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
denoting two
l+1
-th states in some valid hard-to-reason and easy-to-reason CoT sharing the
l
-th state
o_{l}
for task
k
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{REINFORCE}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})>0,

(47)

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{REINFORCE}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0.

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{RAFT}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{RAFT}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})>0,

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{RAFT}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0.

In addition, we have the difference of the logits’ update
\Delta h_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},\bm{o}_{l})-\Delta h_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},\bm{o}_{l})
in Reinforce as:

\begin{aligned} \Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{REINFORCE}}-\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{REINFORCE}}<&\eta[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})]\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\\
&\cdot[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\\
&\cdot\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])]\\
<&0.\\
\end{aligned}

(48)

Also, for RAFT, the difference of logits update
\Delta h_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},\bm{o}_{l})-\Delta h_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},\bm{o}_{l})
is

\begin{aligned} \Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{RAFT}}-\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{RAFT}}<&\eta[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l}))-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l}))]\\
&\cdot\left[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\right]\\
&\cdot\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\\
<&0.\end{aligned}

(49)

2.

Convergence of Finetuning
\&
Constant Error of Pass@K
.

For
\forall\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
, there exists
T\geq\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
, for
t\geq T
, the probability that
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot|{o}_{1})
(trained by REINFORCE or RAFT) reach the
a_{o_{1}}
is converged:

\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}[{o}_{L}^{\prime}=a_{o_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}]\geq\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}[{o}_{L}^{\prime}=a_{o_{1}^{\prime}}^{k},o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}]\geq 1-o(\epsilon).

(50)

Then, suggest the probability mass of valid hard-to-reason CoTs traveling from some
o_{1}\sim P(\mathcal{Q}_{k})
to
a_{o_{1}}
for task
k
in the original TMC
X
(
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}
) is
\Delta
. Then it holds that

\operatorname{Rex}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}(\bm{o})\leq\Theta({(1-\epsilon)\frac{1}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}}+\epsilon\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}}}).

(51)

Further, the
pass@K performance

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{q,k}^{\hat{p}}:=\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{\bm{o}^{i}\}_{i\in[K]}\sim\hat{p}(O\mid q)\\
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{q,k}\end{subarray}}[\bigcup_{i=1}^{K}\mathds{1}\bigl(\bm{o}^{i}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{(k)}\bigr)]
is upper bounded by

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}\leq\underbrace{\Theta([(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}(1-(1-\epsilon)^{K})])}_{\text{Solved by hard CoTs}}+\underbrace{\Theta([(1-(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})(1-\epsilon^{K})])}_{\text{Solved by some easy CoTs}}).

(52)

When
\epsilon=o(\sqrt[K]{1-C_{\mathrm{Err}}/(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}}))\to 0
, the pass@K performance suffer from constant error:
1-\mathrm{Pass@K}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}=\Theta(1)
.

3.

Curriculum Learning of
RL-rej
. For any
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
, suppose setting
G=1
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{rej},(t)}^{o_{1},k}
(Eq.(
46
) excludes all
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})
pairs containing correct CoTs that the current model
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}
predicts with non-trivial probability
\Theta((1-\epsilon)/M)
. Then, optimizing Eq.(
46
via
RL-rej
leads to the following:

(i) The model first learns the easy-to-reason CoTs within
\Theta(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
steps.

(ii) Once its predictive mass over
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
reaches
\Theta((1-\epsilon)/M)
, learning begins on sparse edges in
\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}}=\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
. Hard-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k),\text{hard}}
are progressively learned, with those sharing more edges with
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
being learned earlier.

(iii) Let
\Delta
denote the total probability mass of valid hard-to-reason CoTs from
o_{1}
to
a_{o_{1}}
in the original TMC
X
(under
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}
). Suppose after
T_{2}=\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
, there are
\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}^{\prime}
hard-to-reason CoTs each with likelihood ratio scale
\Theta(\rho)<1
in the
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k)}
have been well-learned with predictive probability
\Theta((1-\epsilon)/M)
. Then the pass@K is at the scale:

\begin{aligned} \mathrm{Pass@K}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}=&\underbrace{\Theta\left[(1-\rho)^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}^{\prime}}(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}}\left(1-(1-\epsilon)^{K}\right)\right]}_{\text{instances unsolvable by learned CoTs}}+\underbrace{\Theta\left[\left(1-(1-\rho)^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}^{\prime}}(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}}\right)(1-\epsilon^{K})\right]}_{\text{instances solvable by some learned CoT}}.\end{aligned}

This bound tends to 1 as
(1-\rho)^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}^{\prime}}\to 0
and
\epsilon\to 0
, showing the superiority of
RL-rej
when the probability mass
\Delta
is non-negligible.

Proof.
Proof of Thm.
10
. In our proofs, we first prove the results of REINFORCE, and the results of RAFT follows directly with a more serious of squeezing effect.

Proof of Item
1
: Difference of Logit Update
.

Recall that by Lemma
9
, we have

\displaystyle\resizebox{22609920}{}{$\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})e_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},{o}_{l}})\right],$}

(53)

\displaystyle\resizebox{22609920}{}{$\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))(e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})e_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},{o}_{l}})\right].$}

Per conditions in our item, there are valid easy-to-reason and hard-to-reason CoTs passing
o_{l}
.

Collaborating Eq.(
53
) with definitions in Item
1
and base model formula in Eq.(
2
), we have

\begin{aligned} \nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\\
\end{subarray}}\Big[&\operatorname{Rex}_{o_{1},k}(\bm{o})\cdot(\mathds{1}(o\in\mathcal{I}_{o_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{(k)})\\
&-\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\mathds{1}(o\in\mathcal{I}_{o_{l+1}^{\prime},o_{l}}^{(k)})\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l}))\Big]\\
=\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\Big[&\mathds{1}(o_{L}=a_{o_{1}}^{k})(p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)\mathds{1}(o\in\mathcal{I}_{o_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{(k)})\\
&-p_{\text{acc}}^{k}({o})\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\mathds{1}(o\in\mathcal{I}_{o_{l+1}^{\prime},o_{l}}^{(k)})\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big]\\
=\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},&o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{o_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr][\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]\\
&-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}]\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l}))]\\
=\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},&o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]\\
&-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\Big]\end{aligned}

(54)

where the first equality is by Eq. (
124
) in Lemma
9
; the second equality is by the definition of
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\cdot)
, Condition (iv) in Def.
2
and
p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(o)
in Eq.(
32
); the third equality is by the condition in our item that
o_{l}\in S_{l},l\in[L-1]
appears as the transition in the valid CoT set as well as the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{o_{l+1},o_{l}}^{(k)}
. Given that for different easy-to-reason CoT sharing
o_{l}
, the
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})
is within the same range, starting from the scale
\Theta(M^{-1})
. Therefore, it is safe to conclude that

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})>0
.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},

\displaystyle o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]

(55)

\displaystyle-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\Big]

Notably, we have the condition in our item that
o_{l}\in S_{l},l\in[L-1]
appears as the transition in the valid CoT set. Then, by Eq.(
32
) as well as the low-probability nature of the sparse edge in Def.
1
, similar to Eq.(
34
) we see that

\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]>\Omega(M\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}])

(56)

Therefore, given that
\mathrm{n}_{q}=O(1)
in Def.
1
as well as
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})\geq\Theta(M^{-1})
, we have

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]>\Omega(\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]).

That is, the rightest term in Eq.(
55
) is strictly lower than
0
, making
\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0
, a serious squeezing effect such that
\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{REINFORCE}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0
. The proof of RAFT is similar–the only difference in Eq.(
53
) is the ultra
(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))
, where the easy-to-reason’s value is larger than the hard-to-reason ones due to the monotonicity of
\log(\cdot)
. Also, noted that
1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\in(1+\log c,1)
, which could be scaling as
O(1)
such that our results of REINFORCE directly applies.

Therefore, it holds that

\begin{aligned} \nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=&\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\Big\{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})\\
&\cdot\Big[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]\\
&-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\Big]\\
&-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]\\
&-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\Big]\Big\}\\
=&(A^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}})-A^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}))\\
&+V^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l})(\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l}))\\
<&\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\\
&\cdot[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})]\\
&\Big[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]\\
&-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\Big]\\
\end{aligned}

(57)

Then, during every update, it holds that
\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{REINFORCE}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})
with
\eta
as the step size. Then we have

\begin{aligned} \Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{REINFORCE}}-\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{REINFORCE}}<\eta&[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})]\cdot\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\\
&\cdot\left[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\right]\end{aligned}

Similarly,

\begin{aligned} \Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{RAFT}}-\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{RAFT}}<\eta&[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l}))-\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l}))]\\
&\cdot\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\\
&\cdot\left[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\right]\end{aligned}

Proof of Item
2
: Convergence and Failure.

Per conditions in our item, there are valid easy-to-reason and hard-to-reason CoTs passing
o_{l}
. Recall that
\bm{\theta}^{k,(0)}=\bm{\theta^{\star}}
at the iteration
t=0
as the base model to be finetuned,
\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}
be the finetuned model for the task
k
at post-training iteration
t
.

For any
w\in(c_{w}/L^{2},o(1/L))
for some small positive constant
c_{w}>0
, we consider the finetuning dynamics during:

\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\leq w,\quad\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\geq 1-w.

(58)

Then for REINFORCE we have the lower bound over the
\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}
for any
{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
as

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}\geq

\displaystyle\eta\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\Theta(\frac{\eta}{M^{L-1}}\cdot\frac{1}{M}\cdot[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}]w-\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]w])

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\Theta(\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}](w-\frac{1}{M}w))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta(\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\frac{(M-1)w}{M}\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}])

For a given TMC
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(\cdot\mid o_{l})=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},\forall l\in[L-1]
, the values of

\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\end{subarray}],\quad\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]

are indeed deterministic positive constants within
(0,1)
, for any valid CoTs
o^{\text{easy}}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
and
o^{\text{hard}}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
passing
o_{l}
. That is, we could omit it in
O(1)
:

\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}\geq\Theta(\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\frac{(M-1)w}{M}).

(59)

Similarly, recall that

D_{o_{l}}=\{o_{l+1}:\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})>0\},\quad c=\min_{o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})>0,

For neuron
{o}_{l+1}^{\text{other}}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
, then by similar derivations we have

\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{other}},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}\leq-\Theta(\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot(\frac{(M+1)w}{M})=-\Theta(\frac{\eta(M+1)w}{M^{L+1}}).

(60)

Therefore, by choosing
T\geq\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
where
0.5>\epsilon>wL>0
is a small constant, we have

\begin{aligned} \sum_{o_{l+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})&=\frac{\sum_{o_{l+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{k,(T)}}}{\sum_{o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{k,(T)}}}=\frac{\sum_{o_{l+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{\star}+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}}}{\sum_{o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{\star}+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}}}\\
&\geq\frac{\sum_{o_{l+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{\star}+T[\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\frac{(M-1)w}{M}]}}{\sum_{o_{l+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{\star}+T[\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\frac{(M-1)w}{M}]}+\sum_{o_{l+1}\in C_{o_{l}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{\star}}+\sum_{o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus C_{o_{l}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{\star}}}\\
&\geq\Theta(\frac{e^{T[\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\frac{(M-1)w}{M}]}}{e^{T[\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\frac{(M-1)w}{M}]}+(M-1)+\sum_{o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus C_{o_{l}}}M^{-1}})\\
&\geq\Theta(\frac{e^{T[\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\frac{(M-1)w}{M}]}}{e^{T[\frac{\eta}{M^{L}}\cdot\frac{(M-1)w}{M}]}+M})=\Theta(\frac{e^{T[\frac{\eta w}{M^{L}}]}}{e^{T[\frac{\eta w}{M^{L}}]}+M})\\
&\geq 1-o(\frac{\epsilon}{L})\\
\end{aligned}

(61)

Here, the first inequality is by the negative updates in Eq.(
47
) and Eq.(
60
) as well as the update lower bound in Eq.(
59
); the second inequality is by dividing
\sum_{o_{l+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}e^{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1},o_{l}}^{\star}}
term,
\lvert\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}\rvert\leq\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}=O(1)
by Def.
1
,
M=\max_{l,o_{l}\in S_{l}}|C_{o_{l}}|
, as well as

\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})}\geq\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}({o}_{l+1}|{o}_{l})}{\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}({o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|{o}_{l})}\geq\Omega(M),

for
\forall{o}_{l+1}\in C_{{o}_{l}},o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus C_{{o}_{l}}
; the third inequality is by the condition in our item
\lvert D_{o_{l}}\setminus C_{{o}_{l}}\lvert=O(M)
; the last inequality is by choosing
T\geq\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
.

Then

\displaystyle\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}[{o}_{L}^{\prime}=a_{o_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}]

\displaystyle\geq\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}[{o}_{L}^{\prime}=a_{o_{1}^{\prime}}^{k},o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}]

(62)

\displaystyle\geq(1-o(\frac{\epsilon}{L}))^{L-1}=1-o(\epsilon).

Therefore, suggest the probability mass of valid hard-to-reason CoTs traveling from some
o_{1}\sim P(\mathcal{Q}_{k})
to
a_{o_{1}}
for task
k
in the original TMC
X
(
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}
) is
\Delta
. Then by Thm.
7
, we have

\operatorname{Rex}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}(\bm{o})\leq\Theta({(1-\epsilon)\frac{1}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}}+\epsilon\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}}}).

(63)

Also, by Thm.
7
the pass@K performance (the probability that at least succeed once among
K
trials) is upper bounded by

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}\leq\underbrace{\Theta([(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}(1-(1-\epsilon)^{K})])}_{\text{upper bound of pass@K of instance that cannot be solved by easy CoTs}}+\underbrace{\Theta([(1-(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})(1-\epsilon^{K})])}_{\text{upper bound of pass@K of instance that can be solved by some easy CoT}}).

Also, by Thm.
7
we see that, when
\epsilon=o(\sqrt[K]{1-C_{\mathrm{Err}}/(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}}))\to 0
, the pass@K performance would suffer from constant error.

The proof of RAFT is similar–the only difference in Eq.(
53
) is the ultra
(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))
, where the easy-to-reason’s value is larger than the hard-to-reason ones due to the monotonicity of
\log(\cdot)
. Also, noted that
1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\in(1+\log c,1)
, which could be scaling as
O(1)
such that our results of REINFORCE directly applies.

Proof of Item
3
: Curriculum Learning of
RL-rej
.

Per Remark
6
, we see that in our case, after
T_{1}\geq\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
for a small
\epsilon
,
RL-rej
would learn valid easy-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
well with non-trivial predictive probability
\Theta((1-\epsilon)/M)
, and start to reject the
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a}^{q,k}
that can be solved by those CoTs.

That is, there exists
T_{1}=\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
, for
t\in(0,T_{1}]
, the
RL-rej
behaves exactly the same with REINFORCE. After
t\geq T_{1}
we have

\begin{aligned} \nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{Rein-rej}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=&\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})\left[0-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\right]<0,\end{aligned}

(64)

for all
{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}},\forall l\in[L-1]
, where the correctly predicted easy-to-reason CoTs are rejected according to the condition in our item. Similarly, for
{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}}=\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}},\forall l\in[L-1]
we have

\begin{aligned} \nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{Rein-rej}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=&\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}\bigl[\bm{o}_{l}^{\prime}=\bm{o}_{l},o_{L}^{\prime}=a_{{o}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}\bigr]\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})\left[\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\hat{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\hat{o}_{L}=a_{\hat{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\hat{o}_{l+1}={o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\end{subarray}]-(\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}[p_{\text{acc}}^{k}(\widetilde{o})\mid\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{o}_{l}=o_{l}\\
\widetilde{o}_{L}=a_{\widetilde{o}_{1}}^{k}\\
\widetilde{o}_{l+1}=o_{l+1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}])\right]>0,\end{aligned}

(65)

where the inequality is by the feeble
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})=o(1/M),o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\sum_{o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}}}
.

Therefore, we have

\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{Rein-rej}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{Rein-rej}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0,\quad\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{Rein-rej}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{Rein-rej}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})>0,

and thus the
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})/\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})
would strictly increase.

By Eq.(
61
), similarly there exists
T_{2}=T_{1}+\Theta(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
, the predictive probability
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(T_{2})}}
of some valid hard-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k),\text{hard}}
would reach
\Theta((1-\epsilon)/M)
. Simultaneously, we see that by adjusting the learning rare to be appropriately small, the predictive probability of easy-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
would not decay below the scale
\Theta((1-\epsilon)/M)
, other wise it would be re-collected in the
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{rej},(t)}^{o_{1},k}
at that iteration
t
according to our condition setting in item
3
. That is, the model
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}
would gradually increase the predictive probability of sparse edges in
\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}}=\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
for
\forall l\in[L-1]
, and thus the CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k),\text{hard}}
sharing the most common edges with some CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k),\text{easy}}
, would first be learned. Afterwards, more and more hard-to-reason CoTs in
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k),\text{hard}}
is getting learned, until the point where further learning a new sparse edge in some
\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{hard}},l\in[L-1]
will make another already learned CoT’s predictive probability to be lower than the scale
o(1-\epsilon)
.

Suppose the probability mass of valid hard-to-reason CoTs traveling from
o_{1}
to
a_{o_{1}}
for task
k
in the original TMC
X
(
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{TMC}}
) is
\Delta
. Suggest after
T_{2}=\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
, there are
\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}^{\prime}
hard-to-reason CoTs each with likelihood ratio scale
\Theta(\rho)<1
in the
\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}}^{(k)}
have been well-learned with predictive probability
\Theta((1-\epsilon)/M)
, then, similar to Thm.
7
, we have

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}=4\underbrace{\Theta([(1-\rho)^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}^{\prime}}(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}}(1-(1-\epsilon)^{K})])}_{\text{upper bound of pass@K of instance that cannot be solved by easy CoTs}}+\underbrace{\Theta([(1-(1-\rho)^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}^{\prime}}(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}})(1-\epsilon^{K})])}_{\text{upper bound of pass@K of instance that can be solved by some easy CoT}})

which would tends to
1
when
(1-\rho)^{\mathrm{n}_{o_{1}}^{\prime}}\to 0,\epsilon\to 0
.

RL-rej
with algorithms other than REINFORCE directly follows.

∎

Theorem 11

(Advantage-based Finetuning Favors Easy-to-Reason CoTs (Formal Version of Thm.
2
))
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
that exact predicts the distribution of a Multi-task TMC as in Def.
1
and
2
, and
\bm{\theta}^{k}
the current model to be finetuned from
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
for task
k\in\mathcal{T}
. Denote the task tuples of task
k\in\mathcal{T}
as
(q,a_{q}^{k},k)
, where
a_{q}^{k}\in S_{L}
is the sole answer state under task
k
. Assume for each
(o_{1},a_{o_{1}},k)
under task
k
, the number of hard-to-reason CoTs from
o_{1}
to
a_{o_{1}}
is bounded by
O(M)
. Let the question distribution during finetuning of task
k
be
P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})
(i.e.,
o_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})
). Suppose the estimates of the RL advantage of PPO / GRPO (without the KL term) by some outer oracle or group-level normalization are accurate during the finetuning:
A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k},\ \hat{A}_{i,l+1}^{k}=A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})
for any CoT
o
, and the
\hat{p}_{\text{old}}^{k}
is appropriately chosen that clip operation
is always functioning
starting from the finetuning with
\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}}=o(1)
such that

\displaystyle(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\leq\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\text{old}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}),\ \text{if }A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0,

(66)

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\leq\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\text{old}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}),\ \text{if }A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\leq 0,

Then the shared form of objective as

\begin{aligned} \mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{o_{1}}}^{o_{1},k},\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}\Big[&\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\}\Big],\end{aligned}

(67)

where
\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}}>0
is a offset clipping parameter, and by Eq.(
20
),

A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})=\mathbb{E}_{o_{1}=q\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}_{k}),\bm{o}_{l+2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\middle|\bm{o}_{l+1}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{o_{1}=q\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}_{k}),\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\middle|\bm{o}_{l}\right].

(68)

Favor Easy CoTs
. For any different state pair
{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\neq{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
denoting two
l+1
-th states in some valid hard-to-reason and easy-to-reason CoT sharing the
l
-th state
o_{l}
for task
k
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{PO}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})>0,\quad\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{PO}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0,

(69)

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{PO}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0,

for
\forall o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
.

There exists
T\geq\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
, for
t\geq T
, the probability that
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot|{o}_{1})
reach the
a_{o_{1}}
is converged:

\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}[{o}_{L}^{\prime}=a_{o_{1}^{\prime}}^{k}]\geq\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}^{\prime}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})\end{subarray}}[{o}_{L}^{\prime}=a_{o_{1}^{\prime}}^{k},o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{o_{1},a_{o_{1}}^{k}}^{(k),\text{easy}}]\geq 1-o(\epsilon).

(70)

Further, the
pass@K performance

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{q,k}^{\hat{p}}:=\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{\bm{o}^{i}\}_{i\in[K]}\sim\hat{p}(O\mid q)\\
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{q,k}\end{subarray}}[\bigcup_{i=1}^{K}\mathds{1}\bigl(\bm{o}^{i}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{(k)}\bigr)]
is upper bounded by

\mathrm{Pass@K}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}\leq\underbrace{\Theta([(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}(1-(1-\epsilon)^{K})])}_{\text{Solved by hard CoTs}}+\underbrace{\Theta([(1-(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}})(1-\epsilon^{K})])}_{\text{Solved by some easy CoTs}}).

(71)

When
\epsilon=o(\sqrt[K]{1-C_{\mathrm{Err}}/(\frac{\Delta M^{L-1}}{1+\Delta M^{L-1}})^{\mathrm{n}_{q}}}))\to 0
, the pass@K performance suffer from constant error:
1-\mathrm{Pass@K}_{o_{1},k}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}}=\Theta(1)
.

Proof.
By Prop.
1
, for each
l
we have

A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\mathrm{easy}})=a_{\mathrm{easy}}^{l}\geq\Theta(M^{-(L+1-l)})>0,\,A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\mathrm{hard}})=-a_{\mathrm{hard}}^{l}\leq-\Theta(M^{-(L+1-l)})<0,

(72)

for constants
a_{\mathrm{easy}},a_{\mathrm{hard}}>0
.

Also, for
\forall o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
, by definition it directly holds that

A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime})<A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\star},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\mathrm{hard}})=-a_{\mathrm{hard}}^{l}<-\Theta(M^{-(L+1-l)})<0.

(73)

Therefore, by Lemma
9
as well as the property of TMC, it holds that

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
