\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\cdot(e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})e_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},{o}_{l}})\right].

Therefore, collaborating with Eq.(
72
) and Eq.(
73
), for
t=0
where
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(0)}}=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}
, we directly have

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{PO}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})>0,\quad\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{PO}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0,

(74)

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},o_{l}}^{k,\mathrm{PO}}:=\eta\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},o_{l}}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})<0,

for
\forall o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
. Indeed, following the proof strategies in Lemma
9
, we directly see that when the transitions of
o_{l}\to o_{l+1}^{\mathrm{easy}}
is further strengthened and the transitions of
o_{l}\to o_{l+1}^{\mathrm{hard}}
is further weaken, the
A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\mathrm{easy}})
is strictly increasing along the iterations, and
A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\mathrm{hard}}),A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}),\forall o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
is strictly decreasing. This makes Eq.(
72
), Eq.(
73
) and Eq.(
69
) hold during the finetuning iterations.

Specifically, for any different state pair
{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}\neq{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}\in\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
and
\forall o_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{o_{l}}^{(k)}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}\geq

\displaystyle\Theta(\eta M^{-(L+1-l)}(1-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus S_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})))>0,

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}\leq

\displaystyle-\Theta(\eta M^{-(L+1-l)}(1+\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus S_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})))<0,

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{\theta}_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},o_{l}}^{k,(t)}\leq

\displaystyle-\Theta(\eta M^{-(L+1-l)}(1+\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in D_{o_{l}}\setminus S_{o_{l}}^{(k),\text{easy}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})))<0,

where the inequalities is by Eq.(
72
), Eq.(
73
), as well as
\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}}=o(1)
.

Similar to the techniques in Thm.
10
, given that
M^{-(L+1-l)}>M^{-L+1}
and
p_{\text{acc}}^{k}\leq 1
, after
T\geq\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
iterations, the remaining proofs and results follows as in Thm.
10
.

∎

Remark 12
.

To simplify the discussion of the policy gradient case and avoid the non-convexity of
\min\{\cdot\}
, we assume the clip operation with
\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}}=o(1)
and Eq. (
66
). However, our results still hold without this assumption.

Specifically, when the
\min
does not select the clipped term, we instead encounter:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\left[\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\text{old}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right]

\displaystyle=2\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\text{old}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})

(75)

\displaystyle=2\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})^{2}}{\hat{p}_{\text{old}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[2\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\text{old}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right],

instead of

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\left[(1\pm\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right]

\displaystyle=(1\pm\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle=(1\pm\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})\mathbb{E}\left[\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right].

Since clearly
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{easy}}|\bm{o}_{l})>\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\text{hard}}|\bm{o}_{l})
, Eq. (
75
) shows that the gradient magnitude for easy edges dominates that of sparse ones. Thus, the squeezing effect persists even without the assumption. We adopt the assumption in our theorem purely to reduce discussion complexity.

Lemma 4
.

[Detailed Version of Lemma
1
] Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
that exact predicts the distribution of a Multi-task TMC as in Def.
1
and
2
, and
\bm{\theta}^{k}
the current model to be finetuned from
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
for task
k\in\mathcal{T}
. Suppose the estimates of RL advantage by GRPO through group-level normalization is accurate as
A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})
for any CoT
o
. The optimal step-wise sampling distribution of the KL-regularized GRPO objective in Eq.(
6
) is:

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\propto\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\exp\left(\hat{r}\frac{A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})}{\beta}\right),

(76)

where
\hat{r}\leq\max\{1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}},c^{-1},\Theta(M)\}
.

Proof.
This result is standard in RL and distribution optimization literature
85
;
34
;
22
;
32
;
21
;
7
;
13
;
64
. The proofs mirror the proof of Corollary
13
in Sec.
H
, and we therefore omit their full proofs for brevity.
∎

Corollary 7

(Full Version of Corollary
2
)
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
that exactly predicts the distribution of a Multi-task TMC as in Defs.
1
and
2
. For any target task
k\in\mathcal{T}
, consider the following two categories of instances:

1.

Istances
(\mathcal{Q},\mathbf{A})
whose correct CoTs only lie in
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}^{k}}^{(k),\text{hard}}
.

2.

Instances
(\mathcal{Q},\mathbf{A})
sampled from another task
k^{\prime}\neq k
.

For PPO/GRPO without KL regularization that satisfy the conditions in Thm.
11
, the pass@K upper bound for these instances after
T\geq\Omega(\eta^{-1}L^{2}M^{L}\log(ML/\epsilon))
is
\left(1-(1-\epsilon)^{K}\right)
.

In contrast, for the optimal sampler
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{\mathrm{PO}}
in Eq. (
76
), for any
\epsilon^{\prime}
satisfying
{1}/{N_{o_{1}}}>\epsilon^{\prime}\geq\epsilon>0
, denote
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}^{k}
as the PPO/GRPO in Thm.
2
with
\epsilon
, if

\beta>\frac{2\hat{r}(L-1)}{\ln\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\prime}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}|D_{o_{l}}|}\right)},

then the pass@K performance of
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{\mathrm{PO}}
is strictly better than that of PPO/GRPO without KL regularization under the same conditions:

1.

Capable of Hard CoTs
: For instance
(\mathcal{Q},\mathbf{A})
with only some hard-to-reason CoTs correct:

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{\mathrm{PO}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})}\Big[R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k}(\bm{o})\Big]\geq\epsilon^{\prime}\geq\epsilon\geq\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}^{k}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})}\Big[R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k}(\bm{o})\Big].

2.

Preserve Multi-task
: For instance
(\mathcal{Q},\mathbf{A})
belonging to untargeted task
k^{\prime}\neq k
:

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{\mathrm{PO}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})}\Big[R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k^{\prime}}(\bm{o})\Big]\geq\epsilon^{\prime}\geq\epsilon\geq\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k,(t)}}^{k}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{1})}\Big[R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k^{\prime}}(\bm{o})\Big].

Proof.

It suffices to prove that with a large
\beta
, any non-zero transition within the TMC is larger than
\epsilon^{\prime}<N_{o_{1}}^{-1}:=|D_{o_{l}}|
.

By the definition of the advantage function in Eq.(
20
, we have

-1\leq A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\leq 1.

Therefore, from Eq.(
76
, the minimum sampling probability over any edge in
D_{o_{l}}
is

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\geq\frac{e^{-\frac{\hat{r}}{\beta}}}{\lvert D_{o_{l}}\rvert e^{\frac{\hat{r}}{\beta}}}=\frac{e^{-\frac{2\hat{r}}{\beta}}}{\lvert D_{o_{l}}\rvert}.

Hence, for any trajectory of length
L
, the probability of sampling a specific terminal state
o_{L}
from any starting state
o_{1}
whose
o_{l+1}
transitions are in
D_{o_{l}}
is lower bounded by

\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\frac{e^{-\frac{2\hat{r}}{\beta}}}{|D_{o_{l}}|}=e^{-\frac{2\hat{r}(L-1)}{\beta}}\cdot\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\frac{1}{|D_{o_{l}}|}.

Define
C:=\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\frac{1}{|D_{o_{l}}|}
. We seek the condition on
\beta
such that this probability is at least
\epsilon^{\prime}
, i.e.,

C\cdot e^{-\frac{2\hat{r}(L-1)}{\beta}}\geq\epsilon.

Dividing both sides by
C
and taking logarithms yields

-\frac{2\hat{r}(L-1)}{\beta}\geq\ln\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{C}\right),\quad\text{so}\quad\beta\geq\frac{2\hat{r}(L-1)}{\ln\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\prime}C}\right)}.

Substituting
C=\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\frac{1}{|D_{o_{l}}|}
, we obtain the desired bound:

\beta>\frac{2\hat{r}(L-1)}{\ln\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\prime}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}|D_{o_{l}}|}\right)}.

That is, the probability of the path
(o_{1},o_{2},\cdots,o_{L}),o_{l+1}\in D_{o_{l}},\forall l\in[L-1]
is larger than
\epsilon^{\prime}
. This ensure that the model is more capable of sampling valid hard-to-reason CoTs for current task as well as valid CoTs for other tasks, as long as the path with transition probability larger than zero (
c>0
) in Def.
1
.
∎

Appendix H
Details and Proofs of Reward-based Sampling

Lemma 5

(BoN/BS with Ground-true Signal Oracle)
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
that exactly predicts the distribution of a Multi-task TMC as defined in Definitions
1
and
2
. Under task tuple
(q,a,k)
, consider the ORMs
R_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{k}(\cdot)
, as well as the PRM given in Eqs.
11
. For any target task
k\in\mathcal{T}
and instance distribution
\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{a,k}
, if the total number of valid hard-to-reason CoTs is
\Theta(M)
, then during pass@K sampling:

•

ORM/PRM-based BoN or BS achieves success probability
\Theta(1)
on task
k
;

•

ORM/PRM-based BoN or BS fails on any other task
k^{\prime}\neq k
.

Proof.
Consider task tuple
(q,a,k)
and an instance
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\in\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{a,k}
that is solvable, i.e., it admits at least one valid CoT in
\mathcal{G}_{q,a_{q}}^{(k)}
. Since the base model
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
assigns
\Theta(c^{L-1})
sampling probability to a correct CoT, the success probability of ORM-based BoN using the ground-truth reward
R_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{k}(\cdot)
satisfies:

\text{pass@K}=\Theta\left(1-(1-c^{L-1})^{NK}\right)=\Theta(1),

where the final equality holds for sufficiently large
K
.

For ORM-based BoN under outcome-population reward
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\cdot)
, the CoT credit depends on relative likelihood. Consider the worst case where there is exactly one correct CoT with success probability
\Theta(c^{L-1})
, while each incorrect but valid CoT has sampling probability
\Theta(1/M^{L-1})
(by Lemma
2
), and dominates
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\cdot)
. Then the probability of sampling the correct CoT at least once in
N
attempts, while avoiding any misleading CoTs, is:

\Theta\left(\left[1-\frac{1}{M^{L-1}}\right]^{N}\cdot\left[1-(1-c^{L-1})^{N}\right]\right).

Hence, the pass@K success probability is lower bounded by:

\Theta\left(1-\left(1-\left[1-\frac{1}{M^{L-1}}\right]^{N}\cdot\left[1-(1-c^{L-1})^{N}\right]\right)^{K}\right)=\Theta(1),

again holding when
K
is large.

Now consider PRM-based BoN under Eq.(
11
. At each step, the minimal success probability is:

\Theta\left([1-\tfrac{1}{M}]^{N}\cdot[1-(1-c)^{N}]\right),

so across
L-1
steps, the overall probability is:

\Theta\left([1-\tfrac{1}{M}]^{N(L-1)}\cdot[1-(1-c)^{N(L-1)}]\right),

and the corresponding pass@K is lower bounded by:

\Theta\left(1-\left(1-[1-\tfrac{1}{M}]^{N(L-1)}\cdot[1-(1-c)^{N(L-1)}]\right)^{K}\right)=\Theta(1).

Now consider any different task
k^{\prime}\neq k
. By Definitions
1
and
2
, the oracle rewards
R_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{k}(\cdot)
, as well as the PRMs in Eqs.
11
, all assign zero credit to instances sampled from
k^{\prime}
. Therefore, all ORM/PRM-based BoN or BS strategies fail on task
k^{\prime}
.

For Beam Search (BS), the result follows by analogous arguments since BS depends on the same reward signals layer-wise.
∎

Proof of Thm.
3
.

Fix any instance
(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})
of task
(o_{1},a,k)
and assume the premise of the theorem: all correct CoTs are hard-to-reason and there exists at least one depth
l^{\star}\in[L]
at which the hard CoTs diverge from a valid easy-to-reason CoT (“sparse edge”). Let
\bm{o}^{\mathrm{easy}}
denote one such easy CoT and
\bm{o}^{\mathrm{hard}}
any hard CoT. By Prop.
2
, population-level ORM and PRM scores strictly prefer the easy branch whenever they differ:

R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}(\bm{o}^{\mathrm{easy}})\;>\;R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}(\bm{o}^{\mathrm{hard}}),\qquad R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}(\bm{o}^{\mathrm{easy}}_{l})\;>\;R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}(\bm{o}^{\mathrm{hard}}_{l})\quad\text{for all $l$ with }\bm{o}^{\mathrm{easy}}_{l}\neq\bm{o}^{\mathrm{hard}}_{l}.

We analyze (i) and (ii)&(iii) separately. Throughout,
M
is the per-node branching factor and
L
is the CoT length. We take the conservative lower bounds that (a) at each node a particular child has sampling probability at least
1/M
, and (b) samples across the
N
trials are i.i.d.

(i) ORM + BoN.

Best-of-
N
(BoN) first draws
N
full trajectories (CoTs) i.i.d. from the generator and then selects the one with the largest ORM score
R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}(\cdot)
. By equation
H
,
if among the
N
samples there exists at least one
\bm{o}^{\mathrm{easy}}
, BoN will select an easy CoT
, hence it will
fail
under the theorem’s premise (easy branch is valid but leads away from any correct hard solution due to the sparse-edge divergence).

We bound the probability that at least one
\bm{o}^{\mathrm{easy}}
appears among
N
samples. Consider any fixed easy CoT
\bm{o}^{\mathrm{easy}}
that agrees with
\bm{o}^{\mathrm{hard}}
on the prefix up to (but excluding)
l^{\star}
and then takes a different child at
l^{\star}
. A conservative lower bound on the probability of sampling this
specific
easy CoT in one draw is

p_{\text{traj}}\;\geq\;\Big(\tfrac{1}{M}\Big)^{L-1}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{M^{L-1}},

since at
L-1
branching decisions (excluding the terminal) we multiply the minimal per-step mass
1/M
. Hence the probability that none of the
N
i.i.d. draws equals this easy trajectory is

(1-p_{\text{traj}})^{N}\;\leq\;\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{M^{L-1}}\Big)^{N}\;=\;\Big(\tfrac{M^{L}-M}{M^{L}}\Big)^{N}.

Therefore, with probability at least
1-(1-1/M^{L-1})^{N}
an easy CoT appears among the
N
draws, and by equation
H
BoN selects it and thus fails. Imposing

\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{M^{L-1}}\Big)^{N}\;\leq\;\epsilon\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad N\;\geq\;\frac{\log(\epsilon)}{\log\!\big(\tfrac{M^{L}-M}{M^{L}}\big)},

ensures that the failure probability is at least
1-\epsilon
, which proves the first bullet.

(ii) PRM + BoN (step-wise) and (iii) PRM + Beam Search (width
N
, beam
B\geq 1
).

PRM-based inference expands
partial
CoTs and uses the local PRM score
R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})
to select among candidates. Consider the first divergence depth
l^{\star}
. In each expansion round at depth
l^{\star}
, the procedure proposes
N
children i.i.d. (BoN: propose and take the best child by PRM; Beam: propose
N
and keep the top-
B
by PRM). Let

p_{\text{child}}\;\geq\;\tfrac{1}{M}

be the conservative lower bound that a given proposal at depth
l^{\star}
takes the (PRM-favored) easy child rather than the hard sparse edge. Thus the probability that
none
of the
N
proposals includes the easy child at that step is

(1-p_{\text{child}})^{N}\;\leq\;\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{M}\Big)^{N}\;=\;\Big(\tfrac{M-1}{M}\Big)^{N}.

Consequently, with probability at least
1-(1-1/M)^{N}
the easy child appears among the
N
proposals at depth
l^{\star}
. By equation
H
, PRM strictly prefers that easy child over the hard child at depth
l^{\star}
, so:

•

PRM + BoN (step-wise):
the chosen next token is the easy child, irrevocably steering the trajectory onto the easy branch. Repeating this argument at later depths where branches differ keeps the easy path strictly preferred, so the final selection is easy and the method fails under the theorem’s premise.

•

PRM + Beam Search:
since
B\geq 1
, any PRM-strictly-better easy child is ranked above the hard child and therefore included in the beam at depth
l^{\star}
; by standard beam monotonicity with strictly better local scores at each subsequent divergence, the easy branch remains in the top-
B
and dominates the final selection, hence failure.

Imposing

\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{M}\Big)^{N}\;\leq\;\epsilon\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad N\;\geq\;\frac{\log(\epsilon)}{\log\!\big(\tfrac{M-1}{M}\big)},

ensures that an easy child appears at the first divergence step with probability at least
1-\epsilon
, and by the PRM preference this forces selection of the easy branch, completing the second bullet.

Conclusion.

In all cases, Prop.
2
ensures a strict scoring advantage for the easy branch whenever it is present among candidates; the displayed lower bounds control the probability that such an easy candidate
does
appear given
N
proposals. Choosing
N
to satisfy

\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{M^{L-1}}\Big)^{N}\leq\epsilon\quad\text{(ORM + BoN)},\qquad\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{M}\Big)^{N}\leq\epsilon\quad\text{(PRM + BoN/BS)},

yields failure probability at least
1-\epsilon
for (i) and for (ii)&(iii), respectively. ∎

Proof.
Heuristic Proof of Corollary
13
. Let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu)
be a base measure space where
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}\ll\mu
with Radon-Nikodym derivative
d\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}/d\mu>0

\mu
-a.e. We consider the optimization over absolutely continuous measures
P_{\text{new}}^{k}\ll\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}
.

The objective functional can be written as:

J(P_{\text{new}}^{k})=\mathbb{E}_{P_{\text{new}}^{k}}[R(\bm{o})]-\frac{1}{\lambda}D_{KL}(P_{\text{new}}^{k}\|\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}})

(77)

where
R(\bm{o}):={R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})
. We require:

(C1)

R\in L^{1}(\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}})
(finite expected reward)

(C2)

\exists\epsilon>0
s.t.
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}\geq\epsilon

\mu
-a.e. (strict positivity)

High-levelly, the remaining proof is convex optimization in probability space. Define the Lagrangian with measure-theoretic notation:

\mathcal{L}(P,\eta)=\int RdP-\frac{1}{\lambda}\int\log\left(\frac{dP}{d\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}\right)dP+\eta\left(1-\int dP\right)

(78)

Require:

(C3)

P\in\mathcal{P}(\Omega)
, the space of probability measures absolutely continuous to
\mu

(C4)

\log(dP/d\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}})\in L^{1}(P)
(finite KL divergence)

For
P\in\mathcal{P}(\Omega)
, consider variation
P_{\epsilon}=P+\epsilon Q
where
Q
is a signed measure with
\int dQ=0
. The Gâteaux derivative is:

\frac{d}{d\epsilon}\mathcal{L}(P_{\epsilon},\eta)\Big|_{\epsilon=0}=\int RdQ-\frac{1}{\lambda}\int\left(\log\frac{dP}{d\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}+1\right)dQ-\eta\int dQ

(79)

For optimality, this must vanish for all admissible
Q
, requiring:

R(\bm{o})-\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\log\frac{dP}{d\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}(\bm{o})+1\right)-\eta=0\quad P\text{-a.s.}

(80)

Rearranging gives:

\log\frac{dP}{d\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}=\lambda R(\bm{o})-(1+\lambda\eta)

(81)

Exponentiating both sides:

dP=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o})\exp(\lambda R(\bm{o}))\exp(-1-\lambda\eta)d\mu(\bm{o})

(82)

Normalization requires:

\exp(1+\lambda\eta)=\int\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}\exp(\lambda R)d\mu=:Z

(83)

Thus the optimal measure is:

dP_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}=\frac{1}{Z}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}\exp(\lambda R)d\mu

(84)

First verify
P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}\in\mathcal{P}(\Omega)
:

•

Absolute continuity: Immediate from
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}\ll\mu
and
Z^{-1}\exp(\lambda R)>0

•

Integrability: By (C1) and
\exp(\lambda R)\leq\exp(\lambda\|R\|_{\infty})<\infty
from
R\leq 1

Second, confirm stationarity. For any
Q\in T_{P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}}\mathcal{P}(\Omega)
(tangent space):

d\mathcal{L}(P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k},\eta)(Q)=\int\underbrace{\left[R-\frac{1}{\lambda}(\log\frac{dP_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}}{d\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}+1)-\eta\right]}_{=0}dQ=0

(85)

Substitute
P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}
into
J
:

\displaystyle J(P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}}[R]-\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathbb{E}_{P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}}\left[\log\frac{P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}}{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}}[R]-\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\lambda\mathbb{E}[R]-\log Z\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\log Z

By Gibbs’ inequality, this maximizes the trade-off between expected reward and KL regularization.

To validate our conditions required, we summarized:

•

(C1): Holds as
\|R\|_{\infty}\leq 1
by assumption

•

(C2): Guaranteed by model construction
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}=\text{softmax}(\cdot)>0

•

(C3): Inherited from base measure
\mu

•

(C4): Satisfied because
D_{KL}(P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}\|\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}})=\log Z-\lambda\mathbb{E}[R]<\infty

Thus under these conditions,
P_{\text{adjusted}}^{k}
is the unique maximizer of
J(P_{\text{new}}^{k})
in
\mathcal{P}(\Omega)
.
∎

Proof.
Proof of the legitimacy of Def.
3
.
To show
h_{k}(\cdot)
is a harmonic function, let us verify

1=\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{new},k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})=\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\frac{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime})}{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})}.

By the fact that

\displaystyle h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right]

(86)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+2:L}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\right]

we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\frac{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime})}{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+2:L}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\right]}{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+2:L}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\right]}{\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+2:L}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\right]}=1

Recall the definition of our
DPRM
:

R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right].\right),

(87)

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{new},k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\frac{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})}{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})}=\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\exp\left(\lambda R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})\right)}{Z_{l}(\bm{o}_{l})},

where
Z_{l}(\bm{o}_{l})=\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\exp\left(\lambda R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime})\right)
. Collaborating with Eq.(
88
) as well as the definition of
R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})
, we can equate:

\exp\left(\lambda R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})\right)=h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}^{\prime}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right].

Therefore, it holds that

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{new},k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})\cdot\frac{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})}{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})}\propto\hat{p}_{{\bm{\theta}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})\exp(\lambda R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})).

Recall:

h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right],

so
h_{k}(\bm{o}_{L})=\exp(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}))
and
h_{k}(\bm{o}_{0})=Z:={\sum_{\bm{o}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{\text{all}}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\right)}
. The h-transformed transition is:

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{new},k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\frac{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})}{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})},

(88)

yielding:

P_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o})=\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\frac{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})}{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})}=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o})\frac{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{L})}{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{0})}=P_{\mathrm{Gibbs}}^{k}(\bm{o}).

(89)

The proof is completed.

∎

Corollary 8
.

For the task
k\in\mathcal{T}
, let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the pretrained Foundation Model from Thm.
8
, and
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\cdot)
be the ORM. Consider the ORM-equipped and DPRM-equipped adjusted sampling distributions defined in Corollary
13
.

•

As the temperature parameter
\lambda\to\infty
, we have the following situation

1.

ORM-Equipped Adjusted Sampling
: The distribution in Eq.(
12
) converges to:

P_{\mathrm{Gibbs}}^{k}(\bm{o})\xrightarrow{\lambda\to\infty}\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\bm{o}=\underset{\bm{o}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{\text{all}}}{\arg\max}\ {R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

akin to a ORM-based
BoN
with
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\cdot)
.

2.

DPRM-Equipped Adjusted Sampling
: The step-wise distribution (
14
) with
R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})=\frac{1}{\lambda}\log h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})
converges to:

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{new},k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})\xrightarrow{\lambda\to\infty}\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\bm{o}_{l+1}=\underset{\bm{o}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}{\arg\max}\ R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

akin to a PRM-based
BoN
with
R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}(\cdot)
.

•

When the temperature parameter
\lambda>0
, each step
l\in\{0,\dots,L-1\}
satisfies:

\argmax_{\bm{o}_{l}\in S_{l}^{\text{BoN}}}R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\argmax_{\bm{o}_{l}\in S_{l}^{\text{BoN}}}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l}),

where
S_{l}^{\text{BoN}}=\{\bm{o}_{l}^{1},\dots,\bm{o}_{l}^{N}\}
denotes the
N
candidates sampled by the base model
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}
. Therefore, using
\lambda>0
with
BoN
,
Beam Search
or
Lookahead Search
equates to prior PRM methods employing the same search strategies.

Proof.
Proof of Corollary
4
. We analyze the asymptotic behavior of the sampling distributions as
\lambda\to\infty
.

For part (1), consider the ORM-equipped adjusted sampling distribution:

P_{\mathrm{Gibbs}}^{k}(\bm{o})=\frac{P_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o})\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right)}{\sum_{\bm{o}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{\text{all}}}P_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})\right)},

where
\mathcal{T}_{\text{all}}
is the set of all possible trajectories. Let
\bm{o}^{*}=\arg\max_{\bm{o}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{\text{all}}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{\prime})
, with maximum reward
R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}(\bm{o}^{*})
. As
\lambda\to\infty
, the term
\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right)
dominates for
\bm{o}
with the largest
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})
. For
\bm{o}\neq\bm{o}^{*}
, if
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})<{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{*})
, then

\frac{\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right)}{\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{*})\right)}=\exp\left(\lambda({R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})-{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{*}))\right)\to 0,

since
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})-{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}^{*})<0
. Assuming
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})
has a unique maximum (or summing over all maximizers if not unique), the denominator is dominated by
P_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}(\bm{o}^{*})\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{k}(\bm{o}^{*})\right)
. Thus,

P_{\mathrm{Gibbs}}^{k}(\bm{o})\to\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\bm{o}=\bm{o}^{*},\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

which matches the behavior of BoN Sampling, where the trajectory with the highest
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{k}(\bm{o})
is selected.

For part (2), consider the DPRM-equipped step-wise distribution:

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{new},k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})\frac{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})}{h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l})},

with
h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l+1}\right]
, and
R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})=\frac{1}{\lambda}\log h_{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})
. Substituting
h_{k}
, we get

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{new},k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})\exp\left(\lambda\left(R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})-R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})\right)\right)\frac{1}{Z},

where
Z=\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})\exp\left(\lambda R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})\right)
is the normalizing constant. Let
\bm{o}_{l+1}^{*}=\arg\max_{\bm{o}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}^{\prime})
. As
\lambda\to\infty
, the term
\exp\left(\lambda R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})\right)
dominates for
\bm{o}_{l+1}=\bm{o}_{l+1}^{*}
. For
\bm{o}_{l+1}\neq\bm{o}_{l+1}^{*}
, if
R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})<R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{*})
, then

\frac{\exp\left(\lambda R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})\right)}{\exp\left(\lambda R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{*})\right)}=\exp\left(\lambda(R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1})-R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{*}))\right)\to 0.

Thus, the distribution concentrates on
\bm{o}_{l+1}^{*}
:

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{new},k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}\mid\bm{o}_{l})\to\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\bm{o}_{l+1}=\bm{o}_{l+1}^{*},\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

which mimics BoN Sampling by selecting the state with the highest
R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}
by our last item.

For the last item, for step
l\in\{0,\dots,L-1\}
, the DPRM with
\lambda>0
is given as
R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\log\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}\left[\exp\left({R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{k}(\bm{o})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right]
. Since
\log
is strictly increasing, we have

\argmax_{\bm{o}_{l}\in S_{l}^{\text{BoN}}}R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\argmax_{\bm{o}_{l}\in S_{l}^{\text{BoN}}}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}\left[\exp\left({R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{k}(\bm{o})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right].

Similarly, since
\exp
is strictly increasing, the
\argmax
over
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}\left[\exp\left({R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{k}(\bm{o})\right)\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right]
is equivalent to the
\argmax
over
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{k}(\bm{o})\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right]
. Thus, given that
R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{\star}}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}^{k}(\bm{o})\mid\bm{o}_{l}\right]
it holds that

\argmax_{\bm{o}_{l}\in S_{l}^{\text{BoN}}}R_{\mathrm{DPRM}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})=\argmax_{\bm{o}_{l}\in S_{l}^{\text{BoN}}}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l+1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta^{\star}}}}R_{\mathrm{likelihood}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l}).

This shows that BoN Sampling with
R_{\text{pro}}^{k}
maximizes the expected outcome reward, aligning with prior methods. The equivalence for Beam Search follows similarly by replacing the sampling strategy with the respective search method, as they also maximize
R_{\text{pro}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l})
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof.
Proof of Cor.
5
. The proof directly follows the proof of Cor.
7
.
∎

Corollary 9

(Extension: Comparison with Ground-true Oracle)
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
that exactly predicts the distribution of a Multi-task TMC as in Definitions
1
and
2
. Under task tuple
(q,a,k)\in S_{1}\times S_{L}\times\mathcal{T}
, consider the ORMs
R_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}}^{k}(\cdot)
and
{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\cdot)
, and the PRMs of Eqs.
11
. For any target task
k
with instance distribution
\mathcal{D}_{a_{q}}^{a,k}
, suppose the number of hard-to-reason CoTs is
\Theta(M)
and the number of nonzero-probability CoTs from
q
to
S_{L}
is
N_{q}
. Then under pass@K sampling:

1.

DPRM is More Capable of Hard CoTs.

If a specific hard CoT has sampling probability
p=o(M^{-(L-1)})
under the base model, then for any BoN budget

N=O\bigl(\tfrac{\log(1-N_{q}^{-1})}{\log(1-p)}\bigr),

there exists

\lambda=o\bigl(\ln\tfrac{(1-p)^{N}}{(N_{q}-1)\,(1-(1-p)^{N})}\bigr)

such that DPRM with temperature
\lambda
achieves strictly higher pass@K than ORM-based or PRM-based BoN (or BS).

2.

Preserve Multi-task.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, if

K=\Omega\Bigl(\frac{\ln\varepsilon}{\ln\bigl(\tfrac{(N_{q}-1)e^{\lambda}}{1+(N_{q}-1)e^{\lambda}}\bigr)}\Bigr),

then DPRM with
\lambda>0
attains pass@K
\geq 1-\varepsilon
on any other task
k^{\prime}\neq k
.

In both cases, adjusting the temperature
\lambda>0
controls the pass@K performance.

Proof.
The arguments parallel those in Cor.
7
, so we focus on the comparison of pass@K success probabilities.

(i) Hard-CoT capability.

Under ORM-based BoN with ground-truth reward, the success probability for the unique hard CoT is

1-(1-p)^{N}.

Under DPRM (Eq.(
12
), every valid CoT—including the correct one—has sampling probability at least

\frac{1}{\,N_{q}-M+Me^{\lambda}\,}\geq\frac{1}{\,1+(N_{q}-1)e^{\lambda}\,}.

Choosing

\displaystyle\lambda=o\bigl(\ln\frac{(1-p)^{N}}{(N_{q}-1)\,(1-(1-p)^{N})}\bigr)

ensures

\tfrac{1}{1+(N_{q}-1)e^{\lambda}}\geq 1-(1-p)^{N}
,
so DPRM outperforms ORM. Similarly, when the budget of PRM-based BoN (or BS) in pass@K is limited and
\lambda\to 0
would achieve more satisfactory success probability.

(ii) Multi-task preservation.

For any other task
k^{\prime}
, DPRM still assigns probability at least
\tfrac{1}{1+(N_{q}-1)e^{\lambda}}
to each valid CoT. Thus, with

K=\Omega\Bigl(\frac{\ln\varepsilon}{\ln\bigl(\tfrac{(N_{q}-1)e^{\lambda}}{1+(N_{q}-1)e^{\lambda}}\bigr)}\Bigr),

the pass@K guarantee

1-\bigl(1-\tfrac{1}{1+(N_{q}-1)e^{\lambda}}\bigr)^{K}\geq 1-\varepsilon

holds, completing the proof.
∎

Appendix I
Auxiliary Lemmas

Lemma 6
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model

\hat{p}_{{\bm{\theta}}}(\cdot|\bm{x})=\operatorname{softmax}(h_{\bm{\theta}}(\cdot,\bm{x})),\ \bm{x}\in\{0,1\}^{\lvert S\rvert}.

(90)

Then for
\forall\bm{o}_{l}\in S_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}\in S_{l+1}

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l}).

(91)

Further, if the base model is Eq.(
2
), we have

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}e_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},{o}_{l}},

(92)

where
e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}:=\bm{o}_{l+1}\bm{o}_{l}^{\top}\in\{0,1\}^{\lvert S\rvert\times\lvert S\rvert}
is the one-hot matrix with only the position corresponding to
({o}_{l+1},{o}_{l})
is
1
and
0
elsewhere.

Proof.
By Eq. (
90
), we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\frac{e^{h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})}}{\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}e^{h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l})}}

(93)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}e^{h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l})}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\frac{\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}e^{h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l})}}{\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}e^{h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l})}}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\frac{\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}e^{h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l})}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l})}{\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}e^{h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l})}}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l}).

Besides, if the base model is
\hat{p}_{{\bm{\theta}}}(\cdot|x)=\operatorname{softmax}(\langle{\bm{\theta}},x\rangle)
by Eq.(
2
), we have

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{\bm{o}_{l+1},\cdot},\bm{o}_{l}\rangle=e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}},

(94)

Dragging Eq.(
94
) into Eq.(
91
), we could obtain Eq.(
92
).

The proof is completed.
∎

Lemma 7

(Policy Gradient for REINFORCE
\&
RAFT under TMC)
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
) that exact predicts the distribution of Multi-task TMC as in Def.
1
and
2
, and
\bm{\theta}^{k}
the current model to be finetuned from
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
for task
k\in\mathcal{T}
. The gradient of the REINFORCE objective for task
k
is given by:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}){R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right],

(95)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}

(96)

\displaystyle\quad\left[(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}){R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right],

(97)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{o_{1}}}^{o_{1},k},\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}\left[R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k}(\bm{o})\right],

(98)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{o_{1}}}^{o_{1},k},\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})R_{(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})}^{k}(\bm{o})\right],

(99)

Remark 13
.

In the main text, Eq.(
4
contains a typo: the summation term “
\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}
” inside the expectation is omitted. The correct formulation is provided in Eq.(
99
. Additionally, the formal versions of Eq.(
7
and Eq.(
8
are given as Eq.(
95
and Eq.(
97
, respectively.

Proof.
For any complete trajectory
\bm{o}=(o_{1},...,o_{L})
:

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o})

\displaystyle=P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(o_{l+1}|o_{l})

(100)

where
P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})
is the initial state distribution (parameter-independent by Def.
1
). By the property of TMC, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}=q\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right]

(101)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{=}}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\left[P^{k}(q)\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right]d\bm{o}_{1:L}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})P^{k}(q)\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right]d\bm{o}_{1:L}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})P^{k}(q)\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right]\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})d\bm{o}_{1:L}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4)}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}

\displaystyle\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(5)}}{{=}}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}\left[\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}){R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right].

Step (1) expands the expectation as an integral over trajectories using the MDP’s joint distribution
P^{k}(q)\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})
;

Step (2) applies the Leibniz interchange under Markovian policy structure:

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})d\bm{o}_{1:L}=\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})d\bm{o}_{1:L}\quad\text{(a.s.)}

(102)

valid when:
(i) Policy Gradient Dominance:
\exists h\in L^{1}(\mu)
such that
\|{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\|\leq h(\bm{o}_{1:L})

\forall\bm{\theta}^{k}\in\Theta^{k}
where
\Theta^{k}=\mathbb{R}^{\lvert S\rvert\times\lvert S\rvert}
denotes the parameter space;
(ii) Parameterized Measure Continuity: The map
\bm{\theta}^{k}\mapsto\sqrt{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})}
is
W^{1,1}
-continuous with:

\lim_{\|\bm{v}\|\to 0}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}+\bm{v}}}-\sqrt{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}}}{\|\bm{v}\|}\right\|^{2}\right]<\infty
, which are all satisfied under our case since
\|{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}=O(1)
and
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|x)=\operatorname{softmax}(\langle{\bm{\theta}},x\rangle)
by Eq.(
2
);

Step (3) decomposes using Markovian parameter isolation:

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\left(\prod_{m=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{m+1}|\bm{o}_{m})\right)\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})

(103)

valid under:
(i) Disjoint Parameter Control:
\bm{\theta}^{k}=\biguplus_{l=1}^{L-1}\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l}
where
\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l}\cap\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l^{\prime}}=\emptyset
for
l^{\prime}\neq l
, with each
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=f_{l}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l};\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l})
and
\frac{\partial f_{l}}{\partial\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l^{\prime}}}\equiv 0
;
(ii) Log-Smoothness:
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})>0

\mu
-a.e. and
\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\in L^{2}(\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\otimes\mu)
;
(iii) Sequential Fubini Condition:
\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})d\bm{o}_{1:L}=\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})d\bm{o}_{l+1}
in terms of total variation norm, which are all easily verified under our
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|x)=\operatorname{softmax}(\langle{\bm{\theta}},x\rangle)
by Eq.(
2
);

Step (4) rewrites the integral as
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}}[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})]
;

Step (5) exchanges summation and expectation via Fubini’s theorem, valid when
\mathbb{E}[|{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}|]<\infty
, which obviously hold in our setting.

Similarly, we have

\begin{aligned} \nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})&=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}=q\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}_{t+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{t})\end{subarray}}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\cdot{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\right]\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{=}}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\cdot P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t=1\\
t\neq l\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})\cdot\Big[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big]d\bm{o}_{1:L}\Bigg)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{=}}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\cdot P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t=1\\
t\neq l\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})\Bigg)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\Big[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big]d\bm{o}_{1:L}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{=}}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\cdot P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\Bigg(\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})\Bigg)\cdot\frac{\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\Big[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big]}{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}d\bm{o}_{1:L}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4)}}{{=}}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}_{t+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{t})\end{subarray}}\Bigg[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\Big(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\Bigg]\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(5)}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\bm{o}_{t+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{t})\end{subarray}}\Bigg[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\Big(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\Bigg]\end{aligned}

(104)

Step (1) expands the expectation using the Markovianity’s factorized structure
P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})
, isolating the
l
-th transition’s
\hat{p}\log\hat{p}
term while keeping others as standard transitions, which is legitimate under our
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|x)=\operatorname{softmax}(\langle{\bm{\theta}},x\rangle)
by Eq.(
2
);

Step (2) enforces parameter-localized differentiation through:

\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\int F_{l}d\bm{o}=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\cdot P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t=1\\
t\neq l\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})\Bigg)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\Big[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\Big]d\bm{o}_{1:L}\quad\text{(a.s.)}

(105)

where
F_{l}={R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\cdot P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t=1\\
t\neq l\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})\cdot[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})]
, valid when:

(i) Architectural Parameter Isolation: Policy parameters partition as
\bm{\theta}^{k}=\biguplus_{l=1}^{L-1}\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l}
with:

\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{\theta}^{k}_{m}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})=\begin{cases}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})&t=l\text{ and }m=l\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(106)

which is satisfied as
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|x)=\operatorname{softmax}(\langle{\bm{\theta}},x\rangle)
by Eq.(
2
); (ii) Localized Dominance:
\exists h_{l}\in L^{1}(\mu_{l})
where
\mu_{l}
is the base measure on
(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})
, such that:

|{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})]|\leq h_{l}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1}),

(107)

which clearly held under our condiions; (iii) Decoupled Integration: For each
l
,

\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}F_{l}d\bm{o}=\int_{\bm{o}_{1}}P^{k}\int_{\bm{o}_{l+1}}[\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}]\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t=1\\
t\neq l\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\int_{\bm{o}_{t+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}d\bm{o}_{t+1}\right)d\bm{o}_{l}d\bm{o}_{l+1}

(108)

with
\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t=1\\
t\neq l\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\int\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}d\bm{o}_{t+1}=1

\mu
-a.e. This condition holds apparently under our model
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|x)=\operatorname{softmax}(\langle{\bm{\theta}},x\rangle)
by Eq.(
2
), which linearly isolates each states; (iv) Transition Differentiability: Each
\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l}\mapsto\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})
is Fréchet differentiable with:
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}\right\|^{2}\right]<\infty

which holds in our softmax model;

Step (3) multiplies one
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})
in the front and divide it subsequently;

Step (4) uses the chain rule:

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))=(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}),

and reconstructs the expectation by recognizing
\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{t}=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})/P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})
, with cross-terms vanishing due to
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{m+1}\sim\hat{p}_{m}}[f(\bm{o}_{l})]=\mathbb{E}[f(\bm{o}_{l})]
for
m\neq l
;
Step (5) applies Fubini’s theorem to exchange summation and expectation, valid by the fact
\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}|(1+\log\hat{p}_{l})\nabla\log\hat{p}_{l}\operatorname{Rex}|\right]<\infty
in our case.

∎

Remark 14
.

When the base model is no longer in the linear form in Eq.(
2
), but a general form in Eq.(
90
) with
\hat{p}_{{\bm{\theta}}}(\cdot|\bm{x})=\operatorname{softmax}(h_{\bm{\theta}}(\cdot,\bm{x})),\ \bm{x}\in\{0,1\}^{\lvert S\rvert}
, the conclusions still holds when

•

Architectural Conditions

–

Parameter Isolation:

\bm{\theta}=\biguplus_{l=1}^{L-1}\bm{\theta}_{l}
where
\bm{\theta}_{l}\cap\bm{\theta}_{l^{\prime}}=\emptyset
for
l\neq l^{\prime}
, with:

h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})=h_{l}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l};\bm{\theta}_{l}),\quad\frac{\partial h_{l}}{\partial\bm{\theta}_{l^{\prime}}}\equiv 0\ \forall l^{\prime}\neq l

(109)

–

Module Independence: Each
h_{l}(\cdot;\bm{\theta}_{l})
uses distinct computational subgraphs without parameter sharing across
l

•

Smoothness & Differentiability

–

Lipschitz Continuity:
\exists C_{l}>0
s.t.

\|h_{l}(\cdot;\bm{\theta}_{l}+\Delta\bm{\theta})-h_{l}(\cdot;\bm{\theta}_{l})\|_{\infty}\leq C_{l}\|\Delta\bm{\theta}\|_{2}\quad\forall\bm{\theta}_{l}

(110)

–

Twice Differentiability:
h_{l}\in C^{2}(\Theta_{l})
with bounded Hessians:

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l}}\left[\|\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\theta}_{l}}h_{l}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\right]<\infty

(111)

•

Gradient Control

–

Bounded Logits:
\exists C<\infty
s.t.

\max_{a}|h_{l}(a,\bm{o}_{l})|\leq C\quad\forall\bm{o}_{l},l

(112)

–

Gradient Norm Bound:

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{l}}\left[\|\nabla_{\bm{\theta}_{l}}h_{l}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq B_{l}<\infty\quad\forall l

(113)

•

Probability Regularity

–

Strict Positivity:
\exists\epsilon>0
s.t.

\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\geq\epsilon\quad\mu\text{-a.e.}\ \forall l

(114)

–

Measure Consistency:

\int_{\mathcal{O}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})d\bm{o}_{l+1}=1\quad\forall\bm{o}_{l},l

(115)

These conditions guarantee: 1. Leibniz rule applicability; through Lipschitz continuity
2. Fubini’s theorem validity via measure consistency;
3. Gradient dominance via bounded logits;
4. Policy smoothness via
C^{2}
differentiability;
5. Numerical stability through strict positivity.

Lemma 8

(Policy Gradient for PO (Eq.(
67
)) under TMC)
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
) that exact predicts the distribution of Multi-task TMC as in Def.
1
and
2
, and
\bm{\theta}^{k}
the current model to be finetuned from
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
for task
k\in\mathcal{T}
. Suggest the accurate
A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})
is available from some outer oracle, the clip operation is always active, and Eq.(
66
) holds. The gradient of the PO objective for task
k
is given by:

\displaystyle\resizebox{22609920}{}{$\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right]$},

(116)

where
r_{l+1}=\frac{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}{\hat{p}_{\text{old}}^{k}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})}
. By the condition that the clip operation is always active, we have

\begin{aligned} \mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k}),(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A})\sim\mathcal{D}_{a_{o_{1}}}^{o_{1},k},\bm{o}_{2:L}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}^{k}(O|\bm{o}_{1})}\Big[&\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\Big],\end{aligned}

(118)

Proof.
It holds that

\begin{aligned} \nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}&=\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}\\
\bm{o}_{t+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\end{subarray}}\left[\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\right]\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})d\bm{o}_{1:L}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{L}}(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\cdot P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})d\bm{o}_{1:L}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}\\
\bm{o}_{t+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\end{subarray}}\left[(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l})\right]\end{aligned}

(119)

The methodologies follow Lemma
8
.

Step (1) expands the expectation using the MDP factorization
P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{t+1}|\bm{o}_{t})
, noting that
\hat{p}_{\text{old}}
is treated as fixed behavioral policy;

Step (2) applies parameter-localized differentiation through:

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{t}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t=1\\
t\neq l\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{\text{old},t}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\hat{p}_{l}

(120)

with conditions similar in Lemma
7
.

Step (3) reconstructs the expectation by recognizing
\prod_{t=1}^{L-1}\hat{p}_{t}=\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{1:L})/P^{k}(\bm{o}_{1})
, leveraging the Markov property.

Key Conditions Inherited from REINFORCE/RAFT in Lemma
7
:

1. Parameter Isolation:
\bm{\theta}^{k}=\biguplus_{l=1}^{L-1}\bm{\theta}^{k}_{l}
with disjoint subparameters
2. Policy Smoothness:
\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\in C^{2}(\Theta)
with bounded Hessians
3. Measure Consistency:
\prod_{t\neq l}\int\hat{p}_{t}d\bm{o}_{t+1}=1

\mu
-a.e.
4. Advantage Regularity:
A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})
is
\sigma(\bm{o}_{1:l+1})
-measurable and bounded.

∎

Based on the policy gradient results, the logit update lemma is provided as below.

Lemma 9
.

Let
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
be the base model in Eq.(
2
) that exact behave like a Multi-task TMC as in Def.
1
and
2
, and
\bm{\theta}^{k}
the current model to be finetuned from
\bm{\theta}^{\star}
for task
k\in\mathcal{T}
. Then

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l}))\right],

(121)

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))(\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l}))\right],

(122)

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l}))\right].

(123)

Further, we have

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})e_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},{o}_{l}})\right],

(124)

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{RAFT}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(1+\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}))(e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})e_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},{o}_{l}})\right],

(125)

\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{PO}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[(1+(2\mathds{1}(A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\geq 0)-1)\epsilon_{\mathrm{clip}})A_{l+1}^{\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}},k}(\bm{o}_{l},\bm{o}_{l+1})\cdot(e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})e_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},{o}_{l}})\right].

(126)

Proof.
By Eq. (
95
) and Eq.(
90
), we have

\begin{aligned} \nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})&=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}){R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right]\\
&=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l}))\right].\end{aligned}

(127)

Similarly By Eq. (
97
), Eq.(
90
), we obtain the results of RAFT.
Given Eq.(
2
), we have

\begin{aligned} \nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})&=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\log\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}|\bm{o}_{l}){R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\right]\\
&=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1},\bm{o}_{l})-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}h_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},\bm{o}_{l}))\right].\end{aligned}

(128)

Given Eq.(
2
), by Eq.(
92
) we have

\displaystyle\resizebox{21479355}{}{$\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{REINFORCE}}(\bm{\theta}^{k})=\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{o}_{1}\sim P^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{k})\\
\{\bm{o}_{l+1}\sim\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\cdot|\bm{o}_{l})\}_{l=1}^{L-1}\end{subarray}}\left[{R_{\mathrm{out}}^{k}}(\bm{o})\cdot(e_{{o}_{l+1},{o}_{l}}-\sum_{\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}\in S_{l+1}}\hat{p}_{\bm{\theta}^{k}}(\bm{o}_{l+1}^{\prime}|\bm{o}_{l})e_{{o}_{l+1}^{\prime},{o}_{l}})\right].$}

The results of RAFT and PO follows.
